\displaystyle\qquad\left|\frac{1}{\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}-z^{\lambda_{1}}}-\frac{1}{\zeta^{\lambda_{2}}-z^{\lambda_{2}}}\right|\,dA(\zeta)+\iint_{\Omega}\frac{\Sigma_{i}|e^{\omega_{i}(z,\zeta,\lambda_{2})}|}{|\zeta^{\lambda_{2}}-z^{\lambda_{2}}|}|\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(Fa)(t)|\,dA(\zeta).

By Hölder’s inequality, this is bounded by

C_{1,0}C_{p}\left\{\Sigma_{i}\sup_{\zeta\in\overline{\Omega}}|\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}\omega_{i}(\cdot,\zeta)|_{0}|F|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+(\Sigma_{i}\|\omega_{i}\|_{0,0})\left(|\Gamma^{\lambda_{1}}-\Gamma^{\lambda_{2}}|_{1}|F|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+|\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}F|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\right)\right\},

where the constants are independent of
\lambda
.
By Lemma
5.4
(iii),
\sup_{\zeta\in\overline{\Omega}}|\omega_{i}(z,\zeta,\lambda_{1})-\omega_{i}(z,\zeta,\lambda_{2})|_{0}\to 0
, so the first term above goes to
0
. Since
\Gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1,0}
, and
F(\cdot,\lambda)
is continuous in
\lambda
in the
L^{p}(\Omega)
-norm, the second and third terms above converge to
0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
.
∎

Step 3: Reduction of the singular integral operator.

To estimate
w_{\eta}^{\lambda}
((
6.8
)), we first need to estimate
\eta(\cdot,\lambda)
.
Write

\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\mathcal{K}_{1}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda),

where

\mathcal{K}_{1}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{b\Omega}Re\left[l(t,\lambda)\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)G_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)\right]\eta(t,\lambda)\,ds,
and

(6.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\lambda}\eta_{1}(\zeta,\lambda):=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{b\Omega}Re\left[l(t,\lambda)l(\zeta,\lambda)(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\overline{G_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}\right]\eta(t,\lambda)\,ds.

The operator
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\lambda}
is compact, since by estimate (
5.5
),

\displaystyle|G_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)|=\left|\frac{e^{\omega_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}\right|\leq C\frac{|\omega_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)-\omega_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)|}{|t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}|}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|\omega_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)|+|\omega_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)|}{t-\zeta}\leq C_{1,0}C_{p}\left(|A(\cdot,\lambda)|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+|B(\cdot,\lambda)|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\right)|t-\zeta|^{-\frac{2}{p}},

where
2<p<\infty
.
We shall call
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\lambda}
a
Fredholm operator of the first kind
, using the same terminologies as in
[
5
]
.

On the other hand, using that
w_{j}(t,t,\lambda)=0
for all
t
and estimate (
5.6
), we have

\displaystyle G_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)-\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\omega_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}+e^{\omega_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}-\frac{2}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\omega_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{1}(t,t,\lambda)}}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}+\frac{e^{\omega_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{2}(t,t,\lambda)}}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1,0}C_{p}\left(|A(\cdot,\lambda)|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+|B(\cdot,\lambda)|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\right)|\zeta-t|^{-\frac{2}{p}}.

It follows that

\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}_{1}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\mathcal{K}_{2}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}_{2}\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{b\Omega}Re\left[\frac{l(t,\lambda)(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\right]\eta(t,\lambda)\,ds;

(6.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{b\Omega}Re\left[l(t,\lambda)\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\sum_{i=1,2}\frac{e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t,\lambda)}}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}\right]\eta(t,\lambda)\,ds.

The operator
\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\lambda}
is compact, and

\mathcal{K}_{2}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{b\Omega}\left[\frac{l(t,\lambda)(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}+\frac{\overline{l(t,\lambda)}\,\overline{(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)}l(\zeta,\lambda)}{\overline{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}}\right]\eta(t,\lambda)\,ds.

Since

\displaystyle\frac{ds}{\overline{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}}=\frac{(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\,d\overline{t^{\lambda}}}{\overline{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}}

\displaystyle=(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\,d\log(\overline{t}-\overline{\zeta^{\lambda}})

\displaystyle=(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\,d\log(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})+(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\,d\log\frac{\overline{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\,dt^{\lambda}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}+(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s)\,d\log\frac{\overline{t^{\lambda}}-\overline{\zeta^{\lambda}}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}},

we can rewrite
\mathcal{K}_{2}^{\lambda}\eta
as

\mathcal{K}_{2}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}_{3}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda),
with

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}_{3}\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\left(l(t,\lambda)\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}+\overline{l(t,\lambda)}l(\zeta,\lambda)\right)\eta(t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}

(6.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{b\Omega}\overline{l(t,\lambda)}l(\zeta,\lambda)\eta(t,\lambda)\,d\,log\frac{\overline{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}.

Now,
\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\lambda}
is a compact operator, and we can write
\mathcal{K}_{3}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}
in the form

\mathcal{K}_{3}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=A(\zeta,\lambda)\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{S(\zeta,t,\lambda)\eta(t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda},

where
A(\zeta,\lambda)\equiv 0
, and

S(\zeta,t,\lambda):=-\frac{i}{4}\,\left(l(t,\lambda)\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}+\overline{l(t,\lambda)}l(\zeta,\lambda)\right).

In particular we have
S(t,t,\lambda)=-\frac{i}{2}
.
Now, the integral equation (
6.10
) can be written as

(6.21)

\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\mathcal{K}_{3}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\lambda}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\gamma_{0}(\zeta,\lambda),

where
\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}_{3}
is a singular integral operator and
N^{\lambda}_{j}
-s are compact operators.
Following
[
5
, P. 118, (45.4)]
, the index of the singular integral operator
\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}_{3}
is defined to be

\frac{1}{2\pi}\left[\arg\frac{A(t,\lambda)-S(t,t,\lambda)}{A(t,\lambda)+S(t,t,\lambda)}\right]_{b\Omega}=\frac{1}{2\pi}[\arg(-1)]=0.

One can find a “ reducing operator”
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}
so that the composition
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}=\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\circ\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}
is a Fredholm operator.
Moreover by Vekua’s equivalence theorem
[
5
, p. 149, case 1]
, if the index of
\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\lambda}
is nonnegative, one can choose
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}
so that the equation

\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\varphi=0

has only the trivial solution
\varphi\equiv 0
. Therefore
\eta^{\lambda}
is a solution to the Fredholm equation
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}=\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\gamma_{0}
if and only if
\eta^{\lambda}
is a solution to the singular integral equation
\mathcal{K}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}=\gamma_{0}
, in other words it suffices to consider the equation

\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}=\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\gamma_{0}^{\lambda}.

There are many choices for such
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}
, for example, one can take
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}
to be the dominant part of the adjoint operator of
\mathcal{K}_{3}^{\lambda}
(See the remark after Definition
7.18
). For an operator
\mathcal{T}
, we use the notation

\mathcal{T}\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=\mathcal{T}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}(\zeta^{\lambda}).

We have

(6.22)

\mathcal{M}\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=-\frac{S(\zeta,\zeta,\lambda)}{\pi i}P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\eta(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}=\frac{1}{2\pi}P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\eta(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}.

Since
S
is Hölder continuous in both
\zeta
and
t
, we can apply formula (
7.15
) to get

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}(\zeta^{\lambda})=\mathcal{M}\mathcal{K}_{3}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle\quad=-[S(\zeta,\zeta,\lambda)]^{2}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)-\frac{S(\zeta,\zeta,\lambda)}{(\pi i)^{2}}\int_{b\Omega}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{S(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{(t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(t^{\lambda}-t_{1}^{\lambda})}\,dt_{1}^{\lambda}\right]\eta(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{4}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)-\frac{i}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{b\Omega}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{l(t,\lambda)\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}+\overline{l(t,\lambda)}l(t_{1},\lambda)}{(t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(t^{\lambda}-t_{1}^{\lambda})}\,dt_{1}^{\lambda}\right]\eta(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda},

where we used that
S(t,t,\lambda)=-\frac{i}{2}
.
We have

(6.23)

\displaystyle\int_{b\Omega}\frac{l(t,\lambda)\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}}{(t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(t^{\lambda}-t_{1}^{\lambda})}=\frac{l(t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}[v_{0}(\zeta,{\lambda})-v_{0}(t,\lambda)],

where we set

\displaystyle v_{0}(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle=P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\overline{l(\tau,\lambda)}}{\tau^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,d\tau^{\lambda}=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\overline{l(\tau,\lambda)}-\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}}{\tau^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,d\tau^{\lambda}+(\pi i)\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}.

By Lemma
7.9
,
v_{0}\in C^{\mu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
and

(6.24)

\|v_{0}\|_{\mu,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|l\|_{\mu,0}.

In view of expression (
6.23
), we have

(6.25)

\mathcal{F}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\frac{1}{4}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\int_{b\Omega}\frac{k_{0}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}{\zeta^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}}\eta(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda},

where
k_{0}(\zeta,t,\lambda)
is given by

(6.26)

k_{0}(\zeta,t,\lambda)=l(t,\lambda)\left(v_{0}(\zeta,\lambda)-v_{0}(t,\lambda)\right).

In view of (
6.24
) we have

(6.27)

|k_{0}(\zeta,t,\lambda)|\leq A_{0}|\zeta-t|^{\mu},

where
A_{0}
is some constant independent of
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
t\in b\Omega
.

Next, we compute the composition operators
\mathcal{M^{\lambda}}\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}
,
\mathcal{M^{\lambda}}\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}
,
\mathcal{M^{\lambda}}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}
. All of them turn out to be Fredholm operators of the first kind. For simplicity, we will omit all the non-zero constants in the following computations. In view of (
6.18
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}_{1}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left[\int_{b\Omega}Re\left[l(t_{1},\lambda)l(t,\lambda)\overline{G_{2}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})\right]\eta(t_{1},\lambda)\,ds_{1}\right]\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{b\Omega}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}Re\left(l(t_{1},\lambda)l(t,\lambda)\overline{G_{2}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})\right)\,dt^{\lambda}\right]\eta(t_{1},\lambda)\,ds_{1}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{b\Omega}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left(l(t_{1},\lambda)l(t,\lambda)\overline{G_{2}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad+\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}\,\overline{l(t,\lambda)}\,\overline{(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})}G_{2}(t,t_{1},\lambda)\left.\right)\,dt^{\lambda}\left.\right]\eta(t_{1},\lambda)\,ds_{1},

where we can easily justify the interchange of integrals.
The inner integral can be expanded as

(6.28)

\displaystyle l(t_{1},\lambda)(t_{1}^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})\int_{b\Omega}\frac{l(t,\lambda)\left(\overline{e^{\omega_{1}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}}-\overline{e^{\omega_{2}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}}\right)}{(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(\overline{t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}})}\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\qquad+\,\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}\,\overline{(t_{1}^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\overline{l(t,\lambda)}\left(e^{\omega_{1}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{2}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}\right)}{(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}\,dt^{\lambda}.

Denoting the first and second integrals in the above expression by
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
respectively, we have

(6.29)

\displaystyle I_{1}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{l(t,\lambda)e^{2iarg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}}{(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}\left[\overline{e^{\omega_{1}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}}-\overline{e^{\omega_{2}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}}\right]\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left\{P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{F(t,t_{1},\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}dt^{\lambda}\right.-P.V.\left.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{F(t,t_{1},\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-t_{1}^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}\right\}.

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}[v^{\prime}_{1}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)-v^{\prime}_{1}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)],

where we set

\displaystyle v^{\prime}_{1}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda):=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{F(t,t_{1},\lambda)-F(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}+\pi iF(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda),

\displaystyle v^{\prime}_{1}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda):=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{F(t,t_{1},\lambda)-F(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-t_{1}^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}+\pi iF(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda),

\displaystyle F(t,t_{1},\lambda):=l(t,\lambda)e^{2iarg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}\left[\overline{e^{\omega_{1}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}}-\overline{e^{\omega_{2}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}}\right].

Note that since
\omega_{1}(t_{1},t_{1})=\omega_{2}(t_{1},t_{1})=0
, we have
F(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)\equiv 0
on
b\Omega
.
We show that
v_{1}^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)\in C^{\nu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
. By Lemma
7.9
, it suffices to see that
F(\cdot,t,\lambda)
is
\nu
-Hölder continuous in the first argument, and this follows from Lemma
7.21
, and estimate (
5.3
). In fact we have

|F(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)|_{\nu}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}|l(\cdot,\lambda)|_{\mu}(\Sigma_{i=1,2}|\omega_{i}(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)|_{\alpha_{p}}),\quad\nu=min(\alpha_{p},\mu),

where the constant is uniform in
\lambda
and
t_{1}\in b\Omega
.
Hence by Lemma
7.9
,

\|v^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|F(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}\|l\|_{\mu,0}(\Sigma_{i=1,2}\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}).

In a similar way, we can show that

(6.30)

I_{2}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)=\frac{1}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}[v^{\prime\prime}_{1}(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda)-v^{\prime\prime}_{1}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)],

where
v^{\prime\prime}_{1}
satisfies

\|v^{\prime\prime}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}\|l\|_{\mu,0}(\Sigma_{i=1,2}\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}).

Putting (
6.29
) and (
6.30
) in (
6.28
), we have

(6.31)

\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}_{1}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{k_{1}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\eta(t_{1},\lambda)\,ds_{1},\quad\quad\nu=min(\alpha_{p},\mu)

where
k_{1}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)
is given by

(6.32)

\displaystyle k_{1}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)

\displaystyle:=l(t_{1},\lambda)(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})[v_{1}^{\prime}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)-v^{\prime}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)]

\displaystyle+\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}\overline{(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})}[v_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)-v^{\prime\prime}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)],

and
k_{1}
satisfies

(6.33)

|k_{1}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)|\leq A_{1}|\zeta-t_{1}|^{\nu}.

Here
A_{1}
is some constant independent of
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
t_{1}\in b\Omega
.
By similar procedures applied to (
6.19
), we can show that the following holds (up to a nonzero constant)

(6.34)

\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}_{2}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{k_{2}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\eta(t_{1},\lambda)\,ds_{1},\quad\quad\nu=min(\alpha_{p},\mu)

where
k_{2}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)
is given by

(6.35)

\displaystyle k_{2}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)

\displaystyle:=l(t_{1},\lambda)(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})[v_{2}^{\prime}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)-v^{\prime}_{2}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)]

\displaystyle+\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}\overline{(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{1})}[v_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)-v^{\prime\prime}_{2}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)],

where
v_{2}^{\prime}
and
v_{2}^{\prime\prime}
satisfy the estimates

\displaystyle\|v^{\prime}_{2}(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)\|_{\nu,0},\>\|v^{\prime\prime}_{2}(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}\|l\|_{\mu,0}(\Sigma_{i=1,2}\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}).

Hence

(6.36)

|k_{2}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)|\leq A_{2}|\zeta-t_{1}|^{\nu},

where
A_{2}
is some constant independent of
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
t_{1}\in b\Omega
.
It remains to compute
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}
. From (
6.20
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{3}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{b\Omega}\overline{l(t,\lambda)}l(\zeta,\lambda)\eta(t,\lambda)\,d\,log\frac{\overline{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(-2i)}{4\pi}\int_{b\Omega}\overline{l(t,\lambda)}l(\zeta,\lambda)\eta(t,\lambda)\,d_{t^{\lambda}}\arg(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2\pi}\int_{b\Omega}\overline{l(t,\lambda)}l(\zeta,\lambda)\eta(t,\lambda)\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s)}\arg(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})\,ds,

where
\tau^{\lambda}
is the unit tangent vector of
b\Omega^{\lambda}
at
t^{\lambda}
.
Omitting the constant in the front, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}_{3}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}l(t,\lambda)[\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})]\eta(t_{1},\lambda)\,ds_{1}\right]\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\int_{b\Omega}\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{l(t,\lambda)[\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})](t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}{(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}\,dt^{\lambda}\right]\eta(t_{1},\lambda)\,ds_{1}.

Denote

F(t,t_{1},\lambda)=l(t,\lambda)(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})[\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})].

As before we show that
|F(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)|_{\mu}
is bounded uniformly in
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
t_{1}\in b\Omega
. Since
l^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\mu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, we only need to show the function

[\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})](t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})

is Hölder
\mu
continuous in
t
, and the Hölder norm is uniformly bounded in
\lambda
and
t_{1}
.

If
|t-t_{1}|<2|t-\widetilde{t}|
, then
|\widetilde{t}-t_{1}|\leq|\widetilde{t}-t|+|t-t_{1}|\leq 3|t-\widetilde{t}|
, and we have

\displaystyle\left|[\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})](t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})-\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})](t_{1}^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})\right|

\displaystyle\quad=\left|\partial{}_{s_{1}}\arg(\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})-t^{\lambda})(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})-[\partial{}_{s_{1}}\arg(\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})](t_{1}^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\partial{}_{s_{1}}\arg(\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})-t^{\lambda})\right||t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|+\left|\partial{}_{s_{1}}\arg(\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})\right||t_{1}^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda}|.

By Lemma
7.21
(i), the above is bounded by

\displaystyle C_{1+\mu,0}\left(|t_{1}-t|^{\mu-1}|t_{1}-t|+|t_{1}-\widetilde{t}|^{\mu-1}|t_{1}-\widetilde{t}|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{1+\mu,0}(|t_{1}-t|^{\mu}+|t_{1}-\widetilde{t}|^{\mu})=C_{1+\mu,0}^{\prime}|t-\widetilde{t}|^{\mu}.

On the other hand if
|t-t_{1}|\geq 2|t-\widetilde{t}|
, we have

\displaystyle\left|[\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})](t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})-\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})](t_{1}^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\left\{\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})-\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})\right\}(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\partial{}_{\tau^{\lambda}(s_{1})}\arg(t_{1}^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})](t^{\lambda}-\widetilde{t}^{\lambda})\right|.

By Lemma
7.21
(ii), since
|t-t_{1}|\geq 2|t-\widetilde{t}|
, the above is bounded by

\displaystyle C_{1+\mu,0}\left(\frac{|t-\widetilde{t}|}{|t-t_{1}|^{2-\mu}}|t_{1}-t|+|t_{1}-t|^{\mu-1}|t-\widetilde{t}|\right)\leq C_{1+\mu,0}^{\prime}|t-\widetilde{t}|^{\mu}.

Now, by the same proof as that for
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\lambda}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\lambda}
, we can show that

(6.37)

\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}_{3}\eta(\zeta,\lambda)=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{k_{3}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\eta(t_{1},\lambda)\,ds_{1},

where
k_{3}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)
is of the form

(6.38)

k_{3}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda):=\overline{l(t_{1},\lambda)}\left(v_{3}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)-v_{3}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)\right),

and
v_{3}
satisfies the estimate

\|v_{3}(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)\|_{\mu,0}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}\|l\|_{\mu,0}.

Hence

(6.39)

|k_{3}(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)|\leq A_{3}|\zeta-t_{1}|^{\mu},

where
A_{3}
is some constant independent of
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
t_{1}\in b\Omega
.

We now apply
\mathcal{M}
to both sides of integral equation (
6.21
). By (
6.25
), (
6.31
), (
6.34
), (
6.37
) we have

(6.40)

(I+\mathcal{N})\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\int_{b\Omega}\frac{k(\zeta,t,\lambda)}{\zeta^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}}\eta(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}=\mathcal{M}\gamma_{0}(\zeta,\lambda),

where
k(\zeta,t,\lambda):=\sum_{i=0}^{3}k_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda)
. By (
6.27
), (
6.33
), (
6.36
) and (
6.39
), we have

|k(\zeta,t,\lambda)|\leq A|\zeta-t|^{\nu},\qquad\nu=\min\left(\mu,\>\alpha_{p}\right),

for some constant
A
independent of
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
t\in b\Omega
.

Step 4: Estimates for the right-hand side of the reduced equation.

We now prove
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\gamma_{0}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu^{\prime},0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
and
\nu=\min(\mu,\alpha_{p})
. Recall that
\gamma_{0}
is given by (
6.11
):

\gamma_{0}(\zeta,\lambda)=\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)-Re[\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}w_{\gamma}^{+}(\zeta,\lambda)]-Re[\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda)}w_{F}(\zeta,\lambda)].

Since for each
\lambda
,
l(\cdot,\lambda)
and
\gamma(\cdot,\lambda)
are Hölder continuous, we can apply the jump formula for generalized Cauchy integrals (Proposition
7.13
) to get

\displaystyle w_{\gamma}^{+}(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,l(\zeta,\lambda)\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)+\frac{1}{2\pi i}P.V.\int_{b\Omega}G_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)l(t,\lambda)\gamma(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}G_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)\overline{l(t,\lambda)}\gamma(t,\lambda)\,\overline{dt^{\lambda}}

\displaystyle:=P_{1}(\zeta,\lambda)+P_{2}(\zeta,\lambda)+P_{3}(\zeta,\lambda)+P_{4}(\zeta,\lambda),

where we set

(6.41)

\displaystyle P_{1}(\zeta,\lambda):=\frac{1}{2}\,l(\zeta,\lambda)\gamma(\zeta,\lambda),\quad\quad P_{2}(\zeta,\lambda):=\pi i[l(\zeta,\lambda)\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)],

(6.42)

\displaystyle P_{3}(\zeta,\lambda):=

\displaystyle\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}\right)[l(t,\lambda)\gamma(t,\lambda)]\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}e^{\omega_{i}(t,t,\lambda)}\right)[l(\zeta,\lambda)\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)]\right\}\,dt^{\lambda},

\displaystyle P_{4}(\zeta,\lambda):=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{e^{\omega_{1}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{2}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}\overline{l(t,\lambda)}\gamma(t,\lambda)\,\overline{dt^{\lambda}}.

By assumption,
P_{1}^{\lambda},P_{2}^{\lambda}\in C^{\mu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
. Using (
6.22
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\overline{l}P_{1})(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\overline{l}(l\gamma)(t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2\pi}P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\gamma(t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left\{\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\gamma(t,\lambda)-\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}+\pi i\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)\right\}.

By Lemma
7.9
, we have

(6.43)

\|\mathcal{M}(\overline{l}P_{1})\|_{\mu,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|\gamma\|_{\mu},\quad\|\mathcal{M}(\overline{l}P_{2})\|_{\mu,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|\gamma\|_{\mu}.

For
P_{3}
, since
\omega_{i}(t,t,\lambda)=0
, we have

(6.44)

\displaystyle P_{3}(\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{l(t,\lambda)\gamma(t,\lambda)}{2(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})}\left[e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t,\lambda)}\right]\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}[l(t,\lambda)\gamma(t,\lambda)-l(\zeta,\lambda)\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)]\,dt^{\lambda},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\overline{l}P_{3})(\zeta,\lambda)=\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\overline{l(t,\lambda)}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{l(t_{1},\lambda)\gamma(t_{1},\lambda)}{2(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}\left[e^{\omega_{i}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{i}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)}\right]\,dt_{1}^{\lambda}\right]\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\overline{l(t,\lambda)}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}}[l(t_{1},\lambda)\gamma(t_{1},\lambda)-l(t,\lambda)\gamma(t,\lambda)]\,dt_{1}^{\lambda}\right]\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega}l(t_{1},\lambda)\gamma(t_{1},\lambda)\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f_{i}(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}\,dt^{\lambda}\right]\,dt_{1}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{b\Omega}\left[\int_{b\Omega}\frac{g(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{(t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda})(t_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})}\,dt^{\lambda}\right]dt_{1}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{l(t_{1},\lambda)\gamma(t_{1},\lambda)}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left[P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f_{i}(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}-\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f_{i}(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-t_{1}^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}\right]\,dt_{1}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left[P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{g(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}-\int_{b\Omega}\frac{g(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-t_{1}^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}\right]\,dt^{\lambda}_{1},

where we denote

f_{i}(t_{1},t,\lambda):=\overline{l(t,\lambda)}\left(e^{\omega_{i}(t,t_{1},\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{i}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)}\right)
and

\displaystyle g(t_{1},t,\lambda):=\overline{l(t,\lambda)}l(t_{1},\lambda)\gamma(t_{1},\lambda)-\gamma(t,\lambda).

We can write
\mathcal{M}(\overline{l}P_{3})
as

(6.45)

\mathcal{M}(\overline{l}P_{3})(\zeta,\lambda)=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{k(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)}{t_{1}^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt_{1}^{\lambda},

where

\displaystyle k(\zeta,t_{1},\lambda)

\displaystyle:=l(t_{1},\lambda)\gamma(t_{1},\lambda)\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(v_{i}(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda)-v_{i}(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)\right)+\left(w(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda)-w(t_{1},t_{1},\lambda)\right),

\displaystyle v_{i}(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda):

\displaystyle=P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f_{i}(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f_{i}(t_{1},t,\lambda)-f_{i}(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}+\pi if_{i}(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda),

\displaystyle w(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda)

\displaystyle:=P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{g(t_{1},t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{g(t_{1},t,\lambda)-g(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}+\pi ig(t_{1},\zeta,\lambda).

Let
\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha,0}:=\sup_{t_{1}\in b\Omega,\lambda\in[0,1]}|\omega_{i}(\cdot,t_{1},\lambda)|_{\alpha_{p}}
. By Lemma
7.9
we have

\displaystyle\|v_{i}(t_{1},\cdot,\lambda)\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|f_{i}(t_{1},\cdot,\lambda)\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}|l(\cdot,\lambda)|_{\mu}.

\displaystyle\|w(t_{1},\cdot,\lambda)\|_{\mu,0}\leq C_{1,0}|g(t_{1},\cdot,\lambda)|_{\mu}\leq C_{1,0}\left(|\gamma(\cdot,\lambda)|_{0}|l(\cdot,\lambda)|_{\mu}+|\gamma(\cdot,\lambda)|_{\mu}\right),

for
\nu=\min(\alpha_{p},\mu)
, where the constants are uniform in
t_{1}\in b\Omega
. It follows from (
6.45
) and Lemma
7.10
that given any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
, one has

(6.46)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}(\overline{l}P_{3})\|_{\nu^{\prime},0}

\displaystyle\leq M_{0},

for some
M_{0}
independent of
\lambda
.
Similarly we can show

(6.47)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}(\overline{l}P_{4})\|_{\nu^{\prime},0}\leq M_{1},

for some
M_{1}
independent of
\lambda
.
Combining (
6.43
), (
6.46
) and (
6.47
), we obtain

(6.48)

\|\mathcal{M}(Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}(w_{\gamma}^{\lambda})^{+}])\|_{\nu^{\prime},0}\leq M,\quad 0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu,

where
M
depends only on
\nu^{\prime}
,
\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}
,
\|l\|_{\mu,0}
and
\|\gamma\|_{\nu,0}
.

Next, we show
\lambda\mapsto\left|\mathcal{M}\left(Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}(w_{\gamma}^{\lambda})^{+}]\right)\right|
is a continuous map from
[0,1]
to
C^{0}(b\Omega)
. For simplicity, we omit the constant
\frac{1}{2\pi}
in the operator
\mathcal{M}
.

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{M}\left(Re\left[\overline{l^{\lambda_{1}}}(w_{\gamma}^{\lambda_{1}})^{+}\right]\right)-\mathcal{M}\left(Re\left[\overline{l^{\lambda_{2}}}(w_{\gamma}^{\lambda_{2}})^{+}\right]\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad=\left|P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{Re[(\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+})(t,\lambda_{1})]}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}\,dt^{\lambda_{1}}-P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{Re[(\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+})(t,\lambda_{2})]}{t^{\lambda_{2}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{2}}}\,dt^{\lambda_{2}}\right|.

We parametrize
b\Omega^{\lambda}
by
\rho^{\lambda}(s)
and set
\zeta^{\lambda}=\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})
. The above is bounded by

(6.49)

\displaystyle\left|(\pi i)\{Re[\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+}](\zeta,\lambda_{1})-Re[\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+}](\zeta,\lambda_{2})\}\right|+\left|\int_{b\Omega}\frac{Re[(\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+})(t,\lambda_{1})]-Re[(\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+})(\zeta,\lambda_{1})]}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}dt^{\lambda_{1}}\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{Re[(\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+})(t,\lambda_{2})]-Re[(\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+})(\zeta,\lambda_{2})]}{t^{\lambda_{2}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{2}}}dt^{\lambda_{2}}\right|.

We can rewrite the above as

(6.50)

\displaystyle\pi\left|\left\{Re[\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+}](\zeta,\lambda_{1})-Re[\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+}](\zeta,\lambda_{2})\right\}\right|+\left|\int_{b\Omega}\frac{F^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(s)-F^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(s_{0})}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}\,ds\right|

\displaystyle+\left.\int_{b\Omega}\left\{Re[(\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+})(t,\lambda_{2})]-Re[(\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+})(\zeta,\lambda_{2})]\right\}\left(\frac{1}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}-\frac{1}{t^{\lambda_{2}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{2}}}\right)dt^{\lambda_{2}}\right|,

where

(6.51)

F^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(s)=Re[\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+}](s,\lambda_{1})(\rho^{\lambda_{1}})^{\prime}(s)-Re[\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+}](s,\lambda_{2})(\rho^{\lambda_{2}})^{\prime}(s),

and we write

Re[\overline{l}w_{\gamma}^{+}](s,\lambda):=Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}(w_{\gamma}^{\lambda})^{+}](t^{\lambda})=Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}(w_{\gamma}^{\lambda})^{+}](\rho^{\lambda}(s)).

We claim that:

(1)

w_{\gamma}^{+}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, that is,

(a)

|w_{\gamma}^{+}(\cdot,\lambda)|_{\nu}
is uniformly bounded in
\lambda
.

(b)

|w_{\gamma}^{+}(\cdot,\lambda_{1})-w_{\gamma}^{+}(\cdot,\lambda_{2})|_{0}\to 0
, as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
.

(2)

For any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
, we have
|F^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}|_{\nu^{\prime}}\to 0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
.

Assuming the above for now, the expression (
6.50
) is then bounded by

\pi\left|\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}Re[\overline{l}w^{+}_{\gamma}]\right|_{0}+C_{1,0}|F^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}|_{\nu^{\prime}}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{ds}{|t-\zeta|^{1-\nu^{\prime}}}+C_{1,0}\|lw_{\gamma}\|_{\nu,0}|\Gamma^{\lambda_{1}}-\Gamma^{\lambda_{2}}|_{1}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{dt}{|t-\zeta|^{1-\nu}}

which converges to
0
uniformly in
\zeta
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
.

We now verify the claims. In view of (
6.51
) and our assumptions on
l^{\lambda}
and
\rho^{\lambda}
, to prove (2) it suffices to show that
|w_{\gamma}^{+}|_{\nu^{\prime}}\to 0
.

By Proposition
2.3
, it suffices to verify claim (1). By (
6.1
) we have

w_{\gamma}^{+}(\zeta,\lambda):=P_{1}(\zeta,\lambda)+P_{2}(\zeta,\lambda)+P_{3}(\zeta,\lambda)+P_{4}(\zeta,\lambda).

By assumption
P_{1},P_{2}\in C^{\nu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, and

(6.52)

\|P_{1}\|_{\mu,0}\leq\|l\gamma\|_{\mu,0},\quad\|P_{2}\|_{\mu,0}\leq\|l\gamma\|_{\mu,0}.

Applying Lemma
7.10
to the expression (
6.44
) for
P_{3}
, we have for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
,

(6.53)

\|P_{3}(\cdot,\lambda)\|_{\nu^{\prime},0}\leq C_{\nu^{\prime}}C_{1,0}\|l\gamma\|_{\mu,0},

where the constants are independent of
\lambda
. In the same way we have

(6.54)

\|P_{4}\|_{\alpha^{\prime},0}\leq C_{\alpha^{\prime}}C_{1,0}\|l\gamma\|_{0,0}

for any
0<\alpha_{p}^{\prime}<\alpha_{p}
. Putting together (
6.52
), (
6.53
) and (
6.54
), we obtain

\|w_{\gamma}^{+}\|_{\nu^{\prime},0}\leq A_{\nu^{\prime}}C_{1,0}\|l\gamma\|_{\mu,0}

for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu<1
. This proves claim (1).

Next we show the continuity of
w_{\gamma}^{+}
in the
C^{0}
-norm.

(6.55)

\left|w_{\gamma}^{+}(\zeta,\lambda_{1})-w_{\gamma}^{+}(\zeta,\lambda_{2})\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}\left|P_{i}(\zeta,\lambda_{1})-P_{i}(\zeta,\lambda_{2})\right|.

In view of (
6.41
), we have

|P_{i}(\zeta,\lambda_{1})-P_{i}(\zeta,\lambda_{2})|\leq C|l(\zeta,\lambda_{1})\gamma(\zeta,\lambda_{1})-l(\zeta,\lambda_{2})\gamma(\zeta,\lambda_{2})|,

for
i=1,2
,
which converges to
0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
, since
l,\gamma\in C^{\mu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
.
By expression (
6.44
) for
P_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle|P_{3}(\zeta,\lambda_{1})-P_{3}(\zeta,\lambda_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{(l\gamma)(t,\lambda_{1})}{2(t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}})}\left(e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda_{1})}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t,\lambda_{1})}\right)(\rho^{\lambda_{1}})^{\prime}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{b\Omega}\frac{(l\gamma)(t,\lambda_{2})}{2(t^{\lambda_{2}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{2}})}\left(e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda_{2})}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t,\lambda_{2})}\right)(\rho^{\lambda_{2}})^{\prime}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}\left\{(l\gamma)(t,\lambda_{1})-(l\gamma)(\zeta,\lambda_{1})\right\}(\rho^{\lambda_{1}})^{\prime}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda_{2}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{2}}}\left\{(l\gamma)(t,\lambda_{2})-(l\gamma)(\zeta,\lambda_{2})\right\}(\rho^{\lambda_{2}})^{\prime}(s)\,ds.

The above expression can be bounded by a sum of the following terms:

(6.56)

\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega}\left\{(l\gamma\rho^{\prime})(s,\lambda_{1})-(l\gamma\rho^{\prime})(s,\lambda_{2})\right\}\frac{e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda_{1})}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t,\lambda_{1})}}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}\,ds,

(6.57)

\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega}(l\gamma)(s,\lambda_{2})\frac{F_{i}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(\zeta)-F_{i}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(t)}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}\,dt^{\lambda_{2}},

(6.58)

\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega}(l\gamma)(s,\lambda_{2})\left(e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda_{2})}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t,\lambda_{2})}\right)\left(\frac{1}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}-\frac{1}{t^{\lambda_{2}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{2}}}\right)\,dt^{\lambda_{2}},

(6.59)

\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\widetilde{F}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(t)-\widetilde{F}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(\zeta)}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}\,ds,

(6.60)

\int_{b\Omega}\left\{(l\gamma)(t,\lambda_{2})-(l\gamma)(\zeta,\lambda_{2})\right\}\left(\frac{1}{t^{\lambda_{1}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{1}}}-\frac{1}{t^{\lambda_{2}}-\zeta^{\lambda_{2}}}\right)\,dt^{\lambda_{2}},

where we set

\displaystyle F_{i}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(\zeta)=e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda_{1})}-e^{\omega_{i}(\zeta,t,\lambda_{2})},\quad\widetilde{F}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(\zeta)=(l\gamma\rho^{\prime})(\zeta,\lambda_{1})-(l\gamma\rho^{\prime})(\zeta,\lambda_{2}).

As before we let
\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}=\sup_{\lambda\in[0,1],t\in b\Omega}|\omega_{i}(\cdot,t,\lambda)|_{\alpha_{p}}
. Then expression (
6.56
) is bounded by

C_{1,0}(\Sigma_{i=1,2}\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0})|\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(l\gamma\rho^{\prime})|_{0}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{|\zeta-t|^{1-\alpha_{p}}}\,ds,\quad\alpha_{p}=\frac{p-2}{p},

which converges to
0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
by assumption.
Also expression (
6.58
) is bounded by

C_{1,0}(\Sigma_{i=1,2}\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0})|\Gamma^{\lambda_{1}}-\Gamma^{\lambda_{2}}|_{1}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{|t-\zeta|^{1-\alpha_{p}}}\,dt,

which converges to
0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
, and expression (
6.60
) is bounded by

C_{1,0}\|l\gamma\|_{\mu,0}|\Gamma^{\lambda_{1}}-\Gamma^{\lambda_{2}}|_{1}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{|t-\zeta|^{1-\mu}}\,dt

which converges to
0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
. Finally in order to show (
6.57
) and (
6.59
) converges to
0
it suffices to see that some Hölder norms of
F_{i}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}
and
\widetilde{F}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}
converge to
0
. This follows from Lemma
2.3
and the following facts:

(1)

|F_{i}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}|_{\alpha_{p}}\leq C\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}
;

(2)

|F_{i}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}|_{0}\to 0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
, by Lemma
5.4
;

(3)

\Gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega})
,
l^{\lambda},\gamma^{\lambda}\in C^{\mu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
.

Hence we have shown that
\lambda\mapsto P_{3}(\cdot,\lambda)
is a continuous map from
[0,1]
to
C^{0}(b\Omega)
. Same can be said for
P_{4}(\cdot,\lambda)
, of which the proof is similar and we leave it to the reader.

Now claim (1) (b) follows from (
6.55
). By earlier remarks this proves that the map

\lambda\mapsto\left|\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\left(Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}(w_{\gamma}^{\lambda})^{+}]\right)\right|
is continuous from
[0,1]
to
C^{0}(b\Omega)
. Combining this with (
6.48
), we have shown that for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
,
\mathcal{M}\left(Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}(w_{\gamma}^{\lambda})^{+}]\right)\in C^{\nu^{\prime},0}(b\Omega^{\lambda}).

Next, we show that
\mathcal{M}\left(Re[\overline{l}w_{F}]\right)\in C^{\nu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(Re[\overline{l}w_{F}])(\zeta,\lambda)=\frac{1}{2\pi}P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{Re[\overline{l}w_{F}]}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left\{\int_{b\Omega}\frac{Re[\overline{l}w_{F}](t,\lambda)-Re[\overline{l}w_{F}](\zeta,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}+\pi iRe[\overline{l}w_{F}](\zeta,\lambda)\right\}.

By Lemma
7.9
, we get

(6.61)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}(Re[\overline{l}w_{F}])\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|lw_{F}\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|l\|_{\mu,0}\|w_{F}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}.

Using estimate (
6.17
) in (
6.61
) we get

(6.62)

\|\mathcal{M}(Re[\overline{l}w_{F}])\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{1,0}C_{p}\|l\|_{\mu,0}(\Sigma_{i=1,2}\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0})\sup_{\lambda\in[0,1]}|F(\cdot,\lambda)|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}.

On the other hand, as we have seen, in order to show that
\mathcal{M}(Re[\overline{l}w_{F}])
is continuous in
\lambda
in the sup norm, it is suffcient to show
w_{F}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha_{p},0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, which is given by Lemma
6.4
. Thus we have proved that

\mathcal{M}\left(Re[\overline{l}w_{F}]\right)\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, and it satisfies estimate (
6.62
). Combining with estimates (
6.48
)
we obtain that for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
,

\|M^{\lambda}\gamma_{0}(\cdot,\lambda)\|_{\nu^{\prime},0}\leq M_{1},

where
M_{1}
is some positive constant depending on
\nu^{\prime}
,
\|\omega_{i}\|_{\alpha_{p},0}
,
\|l\|_{\mu,0}
,
\|\gamma\|_{\mu,0}
, and
\sup_{\lambda\in[0,1]}|F(\cdot,\lambda)|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}
.

Step 5: Estimates for
\eta^{\lambda}

We are now ready to show that
\eta^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
. Recall from (
6.40
) that
\eta(\cdot,\lambda)
satisfies the reduced Fredholm integral equation:

(6.63)

(I+\mathcal{N})\eta(\zeta,\lambda):=\eta(\zeta,\lambda)+\int_{b\Omega}\frac{k(\zeta,t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,\eta(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}=\mathcal{M}\gamma_{0}(\zeta,\lambda),

where
k=\Sigma_{i=1}^{4}k_{i}
, and the
k_{i}
-s are given by (
6.26
), (
6.32
), (
6.35
) and (
6.38
).

Let
w=w_{\eta}(z,\lambda)+w_{\gamma}(z,\lambda)+w_{F}(z,\lambda)
where
w_{\eta}^{\lambda}
,
w_{\gamma}^{\lambda}
and
w_{F}^{\lambda}
are given by formulas (
6.7
) (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) respectively. For
(\zeta,\lambda)\in b\Omega\times[0,1]
, we have
w(\zeta,\lambda)=l(\zeta,\lambda)\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)+il(\zeta,\lambda)\eta(\zeta,\lambda)
. Hence we have the relation: for
(\zeta,\lambda)\in b\Omega\times[0,1]

(6.64)

w_{\eta}(\zeta,\lambda)=l(\zeta,\lambda)\gamma(\zeta,\lambda)+il(\zeta,\lambda)\eta(\zeta,\lambda)-w_{\gamma}(\zeta,\lambda)-w_{F}(\zeta,\lambda).

Now, for each fixed
\lambda
we know by Proposition
3.9
that
\eta^{\lambda}\in C^{\nu}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, and we know that
w_{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda)
is the solution to the following Problem A:

\displaystyle\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{\eta}^{\lambda}(z)+A^{\lambda}(z)w_{\eta}(z)+B^{\lambda}(z)\overline{w_{\eta}^{\lambda}(z)}=0\qquad\text{in $\Omega^{\lambda}$;}

(6.65)

\displaystyle Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}(\zeta)}w_{\eta}^{\lambda}(\zeta)]=\gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta)-Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}w^{\lambda}_{\gamma}](\zeta)-Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}w_{F}^{\lambda}](\zeta)\qquad\text{on $b\Omega^{\lambda}$}.

In addition
w_{\eta}^{\lambda}
satisfies conditions (
6.12
).

We now prove that
\eta^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
. First, we show that
|\eta|_{0}
is bounded in
\lambda
. Seeking contradiction, suppose that for some sequence
\{\lambda_{j}\}
converging to
\lambda_{0}
, such that
N_{j}:=|\eta(\cdot,\lambda_{j})|_{0}\to\infty
.
Let
\widehat{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})=\frac{1}{N_{j}}\eta(\cdot,\lambda)
. Then
|\widehat{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda)|_{0}=1
. Dividing both sides of (
6.63
) by
N_{j}
, we have

(6.66)

(I+\mathcal{N})\widehat{\eta}(\zeta,\lambda_{j})=N_{j}^{-1}\mathcal{M}\gamma_{0}.

The corresponding
w_{\widehat{\eta}}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})
is the solution to the following boundary value problem:

\displaystyle\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{\widehat{\eta}}^{\lambda_{j}}(z)+A(z,\lambda_{j})w_{\widehat{\eta}}(z,\lambda_{j})+B(z,\lambda_{j})\overline{w_{\widehat{\eta}}(z,\lambda_{j})}=0\quad\text{in $\Omega$;}

(6.67)

\displaystyle Re[\overline{l(\zeta,\lambda_{j})}w_{\widehat{\eta}}(\zeta,\lambda_{j})]=\frac{1}{N_{j}}\left\{\gamma(\zeta,\lambda_{j})-Re[\overline{l}w_{\gamma}](\zeta,\lambda_{j})-Re[\overline{l}w_{F}](\zeta,\lambda_{j})\right\}\quad\text{on $b\Omega$},

and in addition
w_{\widehat{\eta}}^{\lambda_{j}}
satisfies the conditions: for
r=1,\dots,N_{0}
and
s=1,\dots,N_{1}
,

(6.68)

\displaystyle w_{\widehat{\eta}}(z_{r},\lambda_{j})=\frac{1}{N_{j}}\left\{a_{r}(\lambda_{j})+ib_{r}(\lambda_{j})-w_{\gamma}(z_{r},\lambda_{j})-w_{F}(z_{r},\lambda_{j})\right\},

(6.69)

\displaystyle w_{\eta}^{+}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{j})=\frac{1}{N_{j}}\left\{l(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{j})(\gamma(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{j})+ic_{s}(\lambda_{j}))-w_{\gamma}^{+}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{j})-w_{F}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{j})\right\}.

By Lemma
7.5
and our earlier estimate for
k
, we have for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
,
|\mathcal{N}\widehat{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})|_{\nu^{\prime}}
is uniformly bounded in
\lambda
. By Arzela-Ascoli theorem, and passing to subsequences if necessary,
\mathcal{N}\widehat{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})
converges to some function in the
C^{\tau}(b\Omega)
norm, for any
0<\tau<\nu
. It follows from equation (
6.66
) that
\widehat{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})
converges to some function
\widetilde{\eta}
in
C^{\tau}(b\Omega)
.

Let

\displaystyle 2\pi w_{\widehat{\eta}}(z,\lambda_{j})=\int_{b\Omega}\left\{G_{1}(z,t,\lambda_{j})l(t,\lambda_{j})\widehat{\eta}(t,\lambda_{j})\,dt^{\lambda_{j}}-G_{2}(z,t,\lambda_{j})\overline{l(t,\lambda_{j})}\widehat{\eta}(t,\lambda_{j})\,\overline{dt^{\lambda_{j}}}\right\},

\displaystyle w_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\lambda_{0}}(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{b\Omega}G_{1}(z,t,\lambda_{0})l(t,\lambda_{0})\widetilde{\eta}(t)\,dt^{\lambda_{0}}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{b\Omega}G_{2}(z,t,\lambda_{0})\overline{l(t,\lambda_{0})}\widetilde{\eta}(t)\,\overline{dt^{\lambda_{0}}}.

From (
6.66
) we have

\widehat{\eta}(\zeta,\lambda_{j})=-\mathcal{N}\widehat{\eta}(\zeta,\lambda_{j})+\frac{1}{N_{j}}\mathcal{M}\gamma_{0}.

By Lemma
7.5
, we have for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
and some constants
C
independent of
\lambda
,

(6.70)

|\widehat{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})|_{\nu^{\prime}}\leq C_{\nu^{\prime}}C_{1,0}|\widehat{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})|_{0}+\frac{1}{N_{j}}\|\mathcal{M}\gamma_{0}\|_{\nu^{\prime}}=C.

Let
\{\{z_{r}\}_{r=1}^{N_{0}},\{z_{s}\}_{s=1}^{N_{1}}\}
be a normally distributed set in
\Omega
. Then it is easy to see that
w_{\widehat{\eta}}(z_{r},\lambda_{i})
converges to
w_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\lambda_{0}}(z_{r})
, since for
z_{r}\in\Omega
, the kernels
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are uniformly bounded in absolute value by a constant.

On the other hand, for
z_{s}^{\prime}\in b\Omega
, we apply the jump formula (
7.6
) to get

\displaystyle w_{\widehat{\eta}}^{+}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,l(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)\widehat{\eta}(z_{s}^{\prime})+P.V.\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}G_{1}(z_{s}^{\prime},t,\lambda)l(t,\lambda)\widehat{\eta}(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}G_{2}(z_{s}^{\prime},t,\lambda)l(t,\lambda)\widehat{\eta}(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}.

In the same way as we did earlier for
w^{+}_{\gamma}
, together with (
6.70
), one can prove that

|w_{\widehat{\eta}}^{+}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{j})-(w_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\lambda_{0}})^{+}(z_{s}^{\prime})|\longrightarrow 0,\quad s=1,\dots,N_{2}

as
|\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{0}|\to 0
.
In fact same proof shows that

|w_{\widehat{\eta}}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})-w_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\lambda_{0}}(\cdot)|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}\longrightarrow 0.

Gathering the results and letting
|\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{0}|\to 0
in (
6.67
), (
6.68
) and (
6.69
), we see that
w_{\widetilde{\eta}}
is the solution to

\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{\widetilde{\eta}}(z)+A^{\lambda_{0}}(z)w_{\widetilde{\eta}}(z)+B^{\lambda_{0}}(z)\overline{w_{\widetilde{\eta}}(z)}=0&\text{in $\Omega^{\lambda_{0}}$;}\\
Re[\overline{l^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta)}w_{\widetilde{\eta}}(\zeta)]=0&\text{on $b\Omega^{\lambda_{0}}$},\end{cases}

satisfying the conditions

w_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\lambda_{0}}(z_{r})=0,\quad(w_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\lambda_{0}})^{+}(z_{s}^{\prime})=0,\quad r=1,...,N_{1},\quad s=1,...,N_{2}.

By Proposition
3.8
, we have
w_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\lambda_{0}}\equiv 0
in
\Omega^{\lambda_{0}}
. By (
6.64
), we have

w_{\eta}(t,\lambda_{j})+w_{\gamma}(t,\lambda_{j})+w_{F}(t,\lambda_{j})=l(t,\lambda_{j})\gamma(t,\lambda_{j})+il(t,\lambda_{j})\eta(t,\lambda_{j}),\quad t\in b\Omega.

Dividing on both sides by
N_{j}
we get

w_{\widehat{\eta}}(t,\lambda_{j})+\frac{1}{N_{j}}w_{\gamma}(t,\lambda_{j})+\frac{1}{N_{j}}w_{F}(t,\lambda_{j})=\frac{1}{N_{j}}l(t,\lambda_{j})\gamma(t,\lambda_{j})+il(t,\lambda_{j})\widehat{\eta}(t,\lambda_{j}),\quad t\in b\Omega.

Letting
|\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{0}|\to 0
, the left-hand side converges uniformly to
w_{\widetilde{\eta}}^{\lambda_{0}}\equiv 0
. Hence the right-hand side converges uniformly to
0
. Since
|l(t,\lambda_{j})|
is bounded below by a positive constant uniform in
t
and
\lambda
, and
\widehat{\eta}(t,\lambda_{j})
converges uniformly to
\widetilde{\eta}
, we conclude that
\widetilde{\eta}\equiv 0
.

On the other hand we have
|\widetilde{\eta}|_{0}=\lim_{j\to\infty}|\widehat{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})|_{0}=1
, which is a contradiction. This shows that
|\eta(\cdot,\lambda)|_{0}
is bounded in
\lambda
. It then follows by (
6.63
), which can be written as
\eta^{\lambda}=-\mathcal{N}^{\lambda}\eta^{\lambda}+\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}\gamma_{0}^{\lambda}
, and Lemma
7.10
that
for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
,

\displaystyle\|\eta\|_{\nu^{\prime},0}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{N}\eta\|_{\nu^{\prime},0}+\|\mathcal{M}\gamma_{0}\|_{\nu,0}\leq C_{\nu^{\prime}}C_{1,0}\|\eta\|_{0,0}.

Next we show
\eta(\cdot,\lambda)
is continuous in
\lambda
in the
C^{0}(b\Omega)
-norm. Suppose this is not the case, then there exists some
\lambda_{0}
and a sequence
\lambda_{j}\to\lambda_{0}
, such that
|\eta(\cdot,\lambda_{j})-\eta(\cdot,\lambda_{0})|_{0}\geq\varepsilon>0
.
Since we have shown that
|\eta(\cdot,\lambda)|_{\nu^{\prime}}
is bounded for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
, passing to a subsequence if necessary, we have that
\eta(\cdot,\lambda_{j})
converges to some
\eta^{\ast}
in the
C^{\nu^{\prime}}(b\Omega)
-norm.
As before we can show that
w_{\eta}(\zeta,\lambda_{0})
converges to
w_{\eta_{\ast}}(\zeta)
uniformly on
b\Omega
. Letting
|\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{0}|\to 0
in (
6.65
) and (
6.12
), we see that
w_{\eta_{\ast}}
is a solution to the following Problem A:

(6.71)

\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{\eta_{\ast}}(z)+A^{\lambda_{0}}(z)w_{\eta_{\ast}}(z)+B^{\lambda_{0}}(z)\overline{w_{\eta_{\ast}}(z)}=0,\quad\text{in $\Omega$};\\
Re[\overline{l^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta)}w_{\eta_{\ast}}(\zeta)]=\gamma(\zeta,\lambda_{0})-Re[\overline{l^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta)}w^{\lambda_{0}}_{\gamma}(\zeta)]-Re[\overline{l^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta)}w^{\lambda_{0}}_{F}(\zeta)],\quad\text{on $b\Omega$},\end{cases}

and in addition
w_{\eta_{\ast}}
satisfies the condition:

\displaystyle w_{\eta_{\ast}}(z_{r}^{\lambda_{0}})=a_{r}(\lambda_{0})+ib_{r}(\lambda)-w_{\gamma}(z_{r},\lambda_{0})-w_{F}(z_{r},\lambda_{0}),\quad r=1,...,N_{1};

\displaystyle w_{\eta_{\ast}}^{+}(z_{s}^{\lambda_{0}})=l(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{0})(\gamma(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{0})+ic_{s}(\lambda_{0}))-w_{\gamma}^{+}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{0})-w_{F}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda_{0}),\quad s=1,...,N_{2}.

On the other hand, we have assumed that
\eta(\cdot,\lambda_{0})
is also a solution to (
6.71
) satisfying the above conditions. Hence by Proposition
3.8
we have
w_{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{0})=w_{\eta_{\ast}}(\cdot)
. Now by (
6.64
), we have for
\zeta\in b\Omega

(6.72)

\displaystyle w_{\eta}(\zeta,\lambda_{0})=l(\zeta,\lambda_{0})\gamma(\zeta,\lambda_{0})+il(\zeta,\lambda_{0})\eta(\zeta,\lambda_{0})-w_{\gamma}(\zeta,\lambda_{0})-w_{F}(\zeta,\lambda_{0});

(6.73)

\displaystyle w_{\eta}(\zeta,\lambda_{j})=l(\zeta,\lambda_{j})\gamma(\zeta,\lambda_{j})+il(\zeta,\lambda_{j})\eta(\zeta,\lambda_{j})-w_{\gamma}(\zeta,\lambda_{j})-w_{F}(\zeta,\lambda_{j}).

By using the fact that
|\eta^{\lambda}|_{\nu^{\prime}}
(
\nu^{\prime}>0
) is uniformly bounded in
\lambda
, we see by the same arguement as before that
w_{\eta}(\cdot,\lambda_{j})
converges uniformly to
w_{\eta^{\ast}}
on
b\Omega
, as
|\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{0}|\to 0
. Passing the limit in equation (
6.73
) we get

w_{\eta}(\zeta,\lambda_{0})=w_{\eta_{\ast}}(\zeta)=l(\zeta,\lambda_{0})\gamma(\zeta,\lambda_{0})+il(\zeta,\lambda_{0})\eta_{\ast}(\zeta)-w_{\gamma}(\zeta,\lambda_{0})-w_{F}(\zeta,\lambda_{0}),\ \zeta\in b\Omega.

Comparing this with equation (
6.72
), we get
\eta_{\ast}(\zeta)=\eta(\zeta,\lambda_{0})
. But
we also have
|\eta^{\ast}(\cdot)-\eta(\cdot,\lambda_{0})|_{0}\geq\varepsilon>0
, which is impossible. Hence we conclude that
\eta(\cdot,\lambda)
is continuous in
\lambda
in the
C^{0}(b\Omega)
norm, and
\eta^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu^{\prime},0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
for any
0<\nu^{\prime}<\nu
.

Now by the proof of Lemma
6.3
in Step 2 one can show that
w^{\lambda}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu^{\prime},0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
.

6.2.
The
\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}
estimate

In this section we prove part (ii) of Theorem
6.1
, namely we show that the solution
w^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu-,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
found in part (i) is in fact in the class
\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.

First we make a reduction to the case
F^{\lambda}\equiv 0
. Recall that
A^{\lambda}
,
B^{\lambda}
and
F^{\lambda}
are taken to be zero outside
\Omega^{\lambda}
. So we can write

(6.74)

w_{F}(z,\lambda)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}G_{1}(z,\zeta,\lambda)F(\zeta,\lambda)\,dA(\zeta^{\lambda})-\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}G_{2}(z,\zeta,\lambda)\overline{F(\zeta,\lambda)}\,dA(\zeta^{\lambda}).

By Lemma
6.4
,
w_{F}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha_{p},0}(\mathbb{C})
. Furthermore,
w_{F}^{\lambda}
satisfies the equation

\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{F}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})+A^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})w_{F}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})+B^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})\overline{w_{F}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})}=F^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda}),\quad z\in\Omega.

Now let
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}
be the solution to the following boundary value problem

\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{\ast}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})+A^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})w_{\ast}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})+B^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})\overline{w_{\ast}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})}=0&\quad z\in\Omega;\\
Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}w_{\ast}^{\lambda}](\zeta^{\lambda})=\gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta^{\lambda})-Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}w^{\lambda}_{F}](\zeta^{\lambda})&\quad\zeta\in b\Omega,\end{cases}

and in addition
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}
satisfies the conditions:

\displaystyle w_{\ast}(z_{r},\lambda)=a_{r}(\lambda)+ib_{r}(\lambda)-w_{F}(z_{r},\lambda)

\displaystyle w_{\ast}^{+}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)=l(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)(\gamma(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)+ic_{s}(\lambda))-w_{F}(z_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)

on the normally distributed set
\{\{z_{r}\}_{r=1}^{N_{0}},\{z_{s}^{\prime}\}_{s=1}^{N_{1}}\}
. Then any solution to the original boundary value problem (
6.1
) satisfying condition (
6.2
) equal to
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}+w_{F}^{\lambda}
. Since
w^{\lambda}_{F}\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha_{p}}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, it suffices to consider the following boundary value problem for
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}
:

(6.75)

\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{\ast}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})+A(z,\lambda)w_{\ast}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})+B(z,\lambda)\overline{w_{\ast}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})}=0&\quad z\in\Omega;\\
Re[\overline{l^{\lambda}}w_{\ast}^{\lambda}](\zeta^{\lambda})=\gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta^{\lambda})&\quad\zeta\in b\Omega,\end{cases}

where
\gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, for
\nu=\min(\alpha_{p},\mu)
.

We now use an idea of Vekua to reduce problem (
6.75
) to one on simply-connected domain. First we introduce some notations. For a bounded domain
\Omega
whose boundary consists of
m+1
connected component, we denote by
b\Omega_{0}
the outer boundary of
\Omega
, and we let
\Omega_{0}
be the bounded simply-connected domain interior to the contour
b\Omega_{0}
. We denote the boundary of the holes in the interior of
\Omega_{0}
by
b\Omega_{j}
, for
1\leq j\leq m
, and we let
\Omega_{j}
be the unbounded domain exterior to the contour
b\Omega_{j}
.

By (
3.31
), we can write the solution of problem (
6.75
) in the form

(6.76)

w_{\ast}(z,\lambda)=w_{0}(z,\lambda)+\cdots+w_{m}(z,\lambda),\quad\quad z\in\Omega,

where for
0\leq j\leq m
, we set

(6.77)

w_{j}(z,\lambda)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega_{j}}G_{1}(z,t,\lambda)w_{\ast}(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}+G_{2}(z,t,\lambda)\overline{w_{\ast}(t,\lambda)}\,\overline{dt^{\lambda}}.

Fix
j_{0}
with
0\leq j_{0}\leq m
, we claim that for all
j\neq j_{0}
,
w_{j}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(b\Omega_{j_{0}}^{\lambda})
.
Assuming this for now, then
w_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}
is a solution to the boundary value problem

(6.78)

\begin{gathered}\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}(z^{\lambda})+A(z,\lambda)w^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}(z^{\lambda})+B(z,\lambda)\overline{w_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})}=0\quad\text{in $\Omega_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}$;}\\
Re[\overline{l(z,\lambda)}w_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})]=\gamma_{j_{0}}(z,\lambda)\quad\text{on $b\Omega_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}$},\end{gathered}

where

\gamma_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(b\Omega_{j}^{\lambda})
with

\gamma_{j_{0}}(z,\lambda):=\gamma(z,\lambda)-\sum_{j\neq j_{0}}Re[\overline{l(z,\lambda)}w_{j}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})].

If
j_{0}=0
, the above problem reduces to the earlier case on bounded simply-connected domain. For
j_{0}\geq 1
, we need to invert the unbounded domain
\Omega_{j_{0}}
.
We fix some point
a_{j_{0}}
in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Omega_{j_{0}}
such that
\operatorname{dist}(a_{j_{0}},b\Omega^{\lambda}_{j_{0}})\geq c>0
for all
\lambda
.
By a substitution
z^{\lambda}=\varphi(\zeta^{\lambda})=\frac{1}{\zeta^{\lambda}-a_{j_{0}}}
, we have for each fixed
\lambda\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta^{\lambda}}}w_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}\left(\varphi(\zeta^{\lambda}))\right)

\displaystyle=\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}(z^{\lambda})\cdot\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta^{\lambda}}}\overline{\varphi}

\displaystyle=-\overline{\partial{}_{\zeta^{\lambda}}\varphi}A^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})w^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}(z^{\lambda})-\overline{\partial{}_{\zeta^{\lambda}}\varphi}B^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})\overline{w^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}(z^{\lambda})}.

Set
\widetilde{w}_{j_{0}}(\zeta,\lambda)=w^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}(\varphi(\zeta^{\lambda}))
,

\widetilde{A}(\zeta,\lambda)=(\overline{\partial{}_{\zeta^{\lambda}}\varphi})A^{\lambda}(\varphi^{-1}(\zeta^{\lambda}))
,

\widetilde{B}(\zeta,\lambda)=(\overline{\partial{}_{\zeta^{\lambda}}\varphi})B^{\lambda}(\varphi^{-1}(\zeta^{\lambda})
,
\widetilde{l}(\zeta,\lambda)=l^{\lambda}(\varphi(\zeta^{\lambda}))
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{j}(\zeta,\lambda)=\gamma^{\lambda}_{j}(\varphi(\zeta^{\lambda}))
, where
\Gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1+\mu,0}(\mathbb{C})
is the extension of
\Gamma^{\lambda}
.
Problem (
6.78
) then becomes

\displaystyle\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta^{\lambda}}}\widetilde{w}_{j_{0}}(\zeta,\lambda)+\widetilde{A}(\zeta,\lambda)\widetilde{w}_{j_{0}}(\zeta,\lambda)+\widetilde{B}(\zeta,\lambda)\overline{\widetilde{w}_{j_{0}}(\zeta,\lambda)}=0,\quad\text{in $D^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}$};

\displaystyle Re\left[\overline{\widetilde{l}(\zeta,\lambda)}\widetilde{w}_{j_{0}}(\zeta)\right]=\widetilde{\gamma}_{j_{0}}(\zeta,\lambda),\quad\text{on $bD^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}$, }

where
D^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}
(resp.
bD^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}
) is the image of
\Omega^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}
(resp.
b\Omega^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}
), under the map
\varphi^{-1}:z^{\lambda}\mapsto\zeta^{\lambda}=\frac{1}{z^{\lambda}}+a
.
Now,
\{D^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}\}
is a family of bounded simply-connected domains in the class
\mathcal{C}^{1+\mu,0}
.
Note that since
A^{\lambda},B^{\lambda}
vanish outside
\Omega^{\lambda}
and in particular near infinity, we have
\widetilde{A}^{\lambda}\equiv\widetilde{B}^{\lambda}\equiv 0
when
\zeta^{\lambda}
is near
a_{j_{0}}
, and
\widetilde{A}(\cdot,\lambda),\widetilde{B}(\cdot,\lambda)
satisfy the same assumptions as
A(\cdot,\lambda)
and
B(\cdot,\lambda)
.
It is clear that
\widetilde{l},\widetilde{\gamma}_{j_{0}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(bD_{j_{0}}^{\lambda})
.

We can now apply Theorem
4.7
to the family of bounded simply-connected domains
D_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}
, and we get
\widetilde{w}_{j_{0}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(\overline{D_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}})
, for each
0\leq j_{0}\leq m
. (Note that the conditions on the normally distributed set are trivially satisfied since we already know from part (i) that
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu^{-},0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, and hence by Proposition
7.12
one has
w_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu^{-},0}(\overline{\Omega_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}})
.)
Since

w^{\lambda}_{j_{0}}(z^{\lambda})=\widetilde{w}_{j_{0}}(\varphi^{-1}(z^{\lambda})),

we have
w_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(\overline{\Omega_{j}^{\lambda}})
, for each
0\leq j_{0}\leq m
.
In view of (
6.76
), we get
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.

It remains to prove for each
j_{0}
and
j\neq j_{0}
, we have
w_{j}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu,0}(b\Omega_{j_{0}}^{\lambda})
.
First we show that
w_{j}^{\lambda}
is continuous in the
C^{0}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})
-norm. Let
d_{\ell}=\operatorname{dist}(b\Omega_{j},b\Omega_{j_{0}})
and
dt^{\lambda}=a(s,\lambda)ds
.
By (
6.77
), we have for
z\in b\Omega_{j_{0}}
,

\displaystyle|w_{j}(z,\lambda_{1})-w_{j}(z,\lambda_{2})|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{b\Omega_{j}}|\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}G_{i}(z,\cdot)||w_{\ast}(t,\lambda_{1})|\,dt^{\lambda_{1}}+|G_{i}(z,t,\lambda_{2})||\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(w_{\ast})(t)|\,ds

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|w_{\ast}\|_{0,0}\int_{b\Omega_{j}}\frac{\Sigma_{i=1,2}|e^{\omega_{i}(z,t,\lambda_{1})}-e^{\omega_{i}(z,t,\lambda_{2})}|}{|t^{\lambda_{1}}-z^{\lambda_{1}}|}\,dt^{\lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|w_{\ast}\|_{0,0}\int_{b\Omega_{j}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|e^{\omega_{i}(z,t,\lambda_{2})}\right|\left|\frac{1}{|t^{\lambda_{1}}-z^{\lambda_{1}}|}-\frac{1}{|t^{\lambda_{2}}-z^{\lambda_{2}}|}\right|\,dt^{\lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{b\Omega_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|e^{\omega_{i}(z,t,\lambda_{2})}\right|}{|t^{\lambda_{2}}-z^{\lambda_{2}}|}|\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(w_{\ast})(s)|\,ds.

For
z\in b\Omega_{j_{0}}
and
t\in b\Omega_{j}^{\prime}
, we have
|t^{\lambda}-z^{\lambda}|
is bounded below by some constant uniform in
\lambda
. Hence the first term in the above sum is bounded by

C_{1,0}C_{j}\|w_{\ast}\|_{0,0}\sum_{i=1,2}\sup_{t\in b\Omega_{j}}|\omega_{i}(\cdot,t,\lambda_{1})-\omega_{i}(\cdot,t,\lambda_{2})|_{C^{0}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})}.

Similarly, the second term is bounded by

C_{1,0}C_{j}\|w_{\ast}\|_{0,0}\left(\Sigma_{i=1,2}\|\omega_{i}\|_{0,0}\right)|\Gamma^{\lambda_{1}}-\Gamma^{\lambda_{2}}|_{1},

and the third term is bounded by

C_{1,0}C_{j}\left(\Sigma_{i=1,2}\|\omega_{i}\|_{0,0}\right)|\delta^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(w_{\ast}a)|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})},

for
\|w_{\ast}\|_{0,0}=\sup_{t\in b\Omega_{j},\lambda\in[0,1]}|w_{\ast}(t,\lambda)|
,
\|\omega_{i}\|_{0,0}=\sup_{z\in b\Omega_{j_{0}},t\in b\Omega_{j}\lambda\in[0,1]}|\omega_{i}(z,t,\lambda)|
.

By part (i), we have
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu^{-},0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
so in particular

|w_{\ast}^{\lambda_{1}}-w_{\ast}^{\lambda_{2}}|_{0}\to 0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
. Together with (
5.4
), we readily see that the above expressions converge to
0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
. This shows that for any
j\neq j_{0}
,
w_{j}(\cdot,\lambda)
is continuous in
\lambda
in the
C^{0}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})
- norm.

Next, we show that
|w_{j}(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{\nu}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})}
is bounded uniformly in
\lambda
. In fact we will show the boundedness of
|w_{j}(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{\alpha_{p}}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})}
.
Recall that
w_{j}^{\lambda}
is given by (
6.77
):

(6.79)

w_{j}(z,\lambda)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega_{j}}G_{1}(z,t,\lambda)w_{\ast}(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}+G_{2}(z,t,\lambda)\overline{w_{\ast}(t,\lambda)}\,\overline{dt^{\lambda}},

for
z\in\Omega_{j},
where
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are given by

\displaystyle G_{1}(z,t,\lambda)=\frac{e^{\omega_{1}(z,t,\lambda)}+e^{\omega_{1}(z,t,\lambda)}}{t^{\lambda}-z^{\lambda}},\qquad G_{2}(z,t,\lambda)=\frac{e^{\omega_{1}(z,t,\lambda)}-e^{\omega_{1}(z,t,\lambda)}}{t^{\lambda}-z^{\lambda}},

and
\omega_{i}
are given by formula (
5.2
). By the proof of Lemma
5.2
, we can show that

\sup_{t\in b\Omega_{j}}|\omega_{j}(\cdot,t,\lambda)|_{\alpha_{p}}\leq C_{1,0}C(p,\Omega)\left(|A(\cdot,\lambda)|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+|B(\cdot,\lambda)|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\right),\quad 2<p<\infty.

Now for
z\in b\Omega_{j_{0}}
and
t\in b\Omega_{j}
where
j\neq j_{0}
,
|t^{\lambda}-z^{\lambda}|
is bounded below by a positive constant
c_{j}
uniform in
\lambda
. Hence it follows by the above formula that
|G_{i}(\cdot,\lambda,\lambda)|_{C^{\alpha_{p}}(b\Omega_{0})}
is bounded uniformly in
t\in b\Omega_{j}
and
\lambda\in[0,1]
. Since we know that
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\nu-,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, in particular
|w_{\ast}(t,\lambda)|
is
bounded above uniformly in
t\in b\Omega_{j}
and
\lambda\in[0,1]
. (
6.79
) then implies that
|w_{j}(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{\nu}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})}
is bounded uniformly in
\lambda
. This proves the earlier claim and the proof is now complete.

6.3.
Differentiability in space variable: The
\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}
estimate

In this subsection we prove part (ii) of Theorem
6.1
, namely the following:

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{C}
such that
b\Omega
has
m+1
connected components. Let
k
be a non-negative integer and
0<\mu<1
. Let
\Gamma^{\lambda}:\Omega\to\Omega^{\lambda}
be a
\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}
embedding. For each
\lambda
, let
w^{\lambda}
be the unique solution to Problem (
6.1
) on
\Omega^{\lambda}
with index
n>m-1
,
satisfying condition (
6.2
)
on a normally distributed set
\{z_{r}^{\lambda},z_{s}^{\prime}\}
in
\Omega
.
Suppose
A^{\lambda},B^{\lambda},F^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
,
l^{\lambda},\gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, and
a_{r},b_{r},c_{s}\in C^{0}([0,1])
. Then
w^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. Furthermore, there exists some constant
C
independent of
\lambda
such that

(6.80)

\displaystyle\|w^{\lambda}\|_{0,0}\leq C\left(\|F^{\lambda}\|_{0,0}+\|\gamma^{\lambda}\|_{\mu,0}+\sum_{r=1}^{N_{0}}\left(|a_{r}|_{0}+|b_{r}|_{0}\right)+\sum_{s=1}^{N_{1}}|c_{s}|_{0}\right),

\displaystyle\|w^{\lambda}\|_{k+1+\mu,0}\leq C\left(\|F^{\lambda}\|_{k+\mu,0}+\|\gamma^{\lambda}\|_{k+1+\mu,0}+\sum_{r=1}^{N_{0}}\left(|a_{r}|_{0}+|b_{r}|_{0}\right)+\sum_{s=1}^{N_{1}}|c_{s}|_{0}\right).

Proof.

As before we write
A(z,\lambda):=A^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})
,
B(z,\lambda):=B^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})
and
F(z,\lambda):=F^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})
.
Note for fixed
\lambda
,
w^{\lambda}\in C^{k+1+\mu}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
by Proposition
3.9
. By Proposition
2.3
, it suffices to show that
w(\cdot,\lambda)
is continuous in
\lambda
in the
C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})
norm and bounded uniformly in
\lambda
in the
C^{k+1+\mu}(\overline{\Omega})
-norm.

Like before we can make a reduction to the case
F^{\lambda}\equiv 0
. Define
w_{F^{\lambda}}
by formula (
6.74
). For the reduction to work we need to show that
w_{F}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. Now
w_{F}^{\lambda}
satisfies the equation:

(6.81)

\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{F^{\lambda}}+A^{\lambda}w_{F^{\lambda}}+B^{\lambda}\overline{w_{F^{\lambda}}}=F^{\lambda}\quad\text{ in $\Omega^{\lambda}$. }

Since by assumption
F^{\lambda}
is at least Hölder continuous, Lemma
6.4
shows that
w_{F^{\lambda}}\in\mathcal{C}^{1^{-},0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. It then follows from (
6.81
) that

\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{F}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.
For each fixed
\lambda
, we apply Cauchy-Green formula to
w^{\lambda}_{F}
on
\Omega^{\lambda}
to get

w_{F}(z,\lambda)=T_{\Omega^{\lambda}}(\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{F^{\lambda}})+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{w_{F}(t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-z^{\lambda}}dt^{\lambda},\quad z\in\Omega

where we write
w_{F}(z,\lambda)=w_{F^{\lambda}}(z^{\lambda})
. Taking
\partial{}_{z^{\lambda}}
on both sides, we have

(6.82)

\partial{}_{z^{\lambda}}w_{F^{\lambda}}(z^{\lambda})=\Pi_{\Omega^{\lambda}}(\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{F^{\lambda}})+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{w_{F}(t,\lambda)}{(t^{\lambda}-z^{\lambda})^{2}}\,dt^{\lambda},

where
\Pi_{\Omega^{\lambda}}:=\partial{}_{z}T_{\Omega^{\lambda}}
.
Since
w_{F^{\lambda}}\in\mathcal{C}^{1^{-},0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, the second term is in
\mathcal{C}^{1-,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.
Now applying Lemma
7.8
to
\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{F^{\lambda}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, we have
\Pi_{\Omega^{\lambda}}(\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w_{F^{\lambda}})\in\mathcal{C}^{\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.
It then follows from (
6.82
) that
\partial{}_{z^{\lambda}}w_{F^{\lambda}}(z^{\lambda})\in\mathcal{C}^{\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.
This shows that
w_{F^{\lambda}}\in\mathcal{C}^{1+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. We can iterate this argument by using (
6.81
) and (
6.82
) so that eventually we get
w_{F^{\lambda}}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.
This proves the reduction.

The problem now is reduced to showing that if
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}
solves (
6.75
), where
\gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, then
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.
As in the proof of part (ii), we reduce the problem to one on simply-connected domain, and we use the same notation
w_{j}
and
b\Omega^{j}
as before.

Note also that by the assumptions and the result of part (ii),
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\frac{p-2}{p},0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
for any
2<p<\infty
. Hence
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1-,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.

In view of formula (
3.31
) with
F\equiv 0
, we can write the solution of problem (
6.75
) in the form

w_{\ast}(z,\lambda)=w_{0}(z,\lambda)+\cdots+w_{m}(z,\lambda),z\in\Omega,

where we set

w_{j}(z,\lambda)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega_{j}}G_{1}(z,t,\lambda)w_{\ast}(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}+G_{2}(z,t,\lambda)\overline{w_{\ast}(t,\lambda)}\,\overline{dt^{\lambda}}.

Fix
j_{0}
with
0\leq j_{0}\leq m
, we need to show that for all
j\neq j_{0}
, we have
w_{j}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(b\Omega_{j_{0}}^{\lambda})
. The rest of the argument follows the same as in part (ii).

That
w_{j}^{\lambda}
is continuous in the
C^{0}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})
-norm is proved in part (ii),
so we only need to show that
|w_{j}(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{k+1+\mu}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})}
is bounded in
\lambda
. Recall that

w_{j}^{\lambda}
satisfies the equation:

\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w^{\lambda}_{j}(z^{\lambda})+A^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})w^{\lambda}_{j}(z^{\lambda})+B^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})\overline{w_{j}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})}=0,\quad z^{\lambda}\in\Omega_{j}^{\lambda}.

By part (i), we have
w_{\ast}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1^{-},0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. In view of Proposition
7.12
and (
6.77
),

w_{j}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1^{-},0}(\overline{\Omega_{j}^{\lambda}})
.
Fix an open set
V_{j}
so that
V_{j}\subset\subset(\Omega_{j}^{\lambda}\cap\Omega^{\lambda})
and
\operatorname{dist}(b\Omega_{j_{0}}^{\lambda},V_{j})\geq\delta_{0}
for all
\lambda
. Certainly
w^{\lambda}_{j}
satisfy the same equation on
V_{j}
:

(6.83)

\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w^{\lambda}_{j}(z)+A^{\lambda}(z)w^{\lambda}_{j}(z)+B^{\lambda}(z)\overline{w_{j}^{\lambda}(z)}=0,\quad z\in V_{j}.

Here
A^{\lambda},B^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,0}(\overline{V_{j}})\subset\mathcal{C}^{\mu,0}(\overline{V_{j}})
and
w^{\lambda}_{j}\in\mathcal{C}^{1^{-},0}(\overline{V_{j}})
, thus equation (
6.83
) implies that
\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{j}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\mu,0}(\overline{V_{j}})
.

Applying Cauchy-Green formula to
V_{j}
, we have for each fixed
\lambda
,

w_{j}^{\lambda}(z)=T_{V_{j}}(\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{j})(z)+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{bV_{j}}\frac{w_{j}^{\lambda}(\zeta)}{\zeta-z}\,d\zeta,\quad z\in V_{j}.

Taking
\partial{}_{z}
on both sides we get

(6.84)

\partial{}_{z}w_{j}^{\lambda}(z)=\partial{}_{z}T_{V_{j}}(\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{j}^{\lambda})(z)+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{bV_{j}}\frac{w_{j}^{\lambda}(\zeta)}{(\zeta-z)^{2}}\,\,d\zeta,\quad z\in V_{j}.

Now, for
\zeta\in bV_{j}
and
z\in b\Omega_{j_{0}}^{\lambda}
,
|\zeta-z|
is bounded below by a positive constant independent of
\lambda
, so the second term above is bounded by

C|w_{j}^{\lambda}|_{C^{0}(bV_{j})}
for some
C
independent of
\lambda
.
On the other hand by Lemma
7.8
,

|\partial{}_{z}T_{V_{j}}(\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{j}^{\lambda})|_{C^{\mu}(\overline{V_{j}})}\leq C|\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{j}^{\lambda}|_{C^{\mu}(\overline{V_{j}})}.

Thus

|\partial{}_{z}w_{j}^{\lambda}|_{C^{\mu}(\overline{V_{j}})}\leq C\left(|\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{j^{\prime}}^{\lambda}|_{C^{\mu}(\overline{V_{j}})}+|w_{j}^{\lambda}|_{C^{0}(\overline{V_{j}})}\right),

where
C
is some constant independent of
\lambda
.
Hence
|w_{j}^{\lambda}|_{C^{1+\mu}(V_{j})}
is uniformly bounded in
\lambda
.
By repeating the argument using (
6.83
) and (
6.84
), we can show that

|w_{j}^{\lambda}|_{C^{k+1+\mu}(V_{j})}
is uniformly bounded in
\lambda
.
Since
\Gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega})
and
b\Omega_{j_{0}}\subset V_{j}
,
|w_{j}^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})|_{C^{k+1+\mu}(b\Omega_{j_{0}})}
is bounded uniformly in
\lambda
.
This completes the proof of the claim and thus the theorem.
∎

6.4.
Differentiability in parameter: the
\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}
estimate

In this subsection we prove part (iii) of Theorem
6.1
, namely the following:

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
k,j,\mu
be as in Theorem
1.1
.
Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{C}
such that
b\Omega
has
m+1
connected component. Suppose
\Gamma^{\lambda}:\Omega\to\Omega^{\lambda}
is a
\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}
embedding. For each
\lambda
, let
w^{\lambda}
be the unique solution to Problem (
6.1
) on
\Omega^{\lambda}
with index
n>m-1
, satisfying condition (
6.2
)
on a normally distributed set
\{\zeta_{r}^{\lambda},(\zeta_{s}^{\prime})^{\lambda}\}
in
\Omega^{\lambda}
.
Suppose
A^{\lambda},B^{\lambda},F^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
,
l^{\lambda},\gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, and
a_{r},b_{r},c_{s}\in C^{j}([0,1])
. Then
w^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. Furthermore, there exists some constant
C
independent of
\lambda
such that

(6.85)

\|w^{\lambda}\|_{k+1+\mu,j}\leq C\left(\|F^{\lambda}\|_{k+\mu,j}+\|\gamma^{\lambda}\|_{k+1+\mu,j}+\sum_{r=1}^{N_{0}}\left(|a_{r}|_{j}+|b_{r}|_{j}\right)+\sum_{s=1}^{N_{1}}|c_{s}|_{j}\right).

Proof.

We proceed with induction on
j
. The
j=0
case is proved in Theorem (
6.1
) part (ii). Suppose the statement has been proved for
j-1
. In particular we know that
w^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j-1}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
for
k\geq j\geq 1
.

Let
\Psi^{\lambda}=(\Gamma^{\lambda})^{-1}
and
v^{\lambda}(\zeta)=w^{\lambda}\circ\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta)
. Then
w^{\lambda}=v^{\lambda}\circ\Psi^{\lambda}
,
\Psi^{\lambda}(z^{\lambda})=\zeta
. For fixed
\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\partial{}_{\overline{z^{\lambda}}}w^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\Psi^{\lambda}}{\partial\overline{z^{\lambda}}}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta))\partial{}_{\zeta}v^{\lambda}+\frac{\partial\overline{\Psi^{\lambda}}}{\partial\overline{z^{\lambda}}}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta))\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}v^{\lambda}=h^{\lambda}(\zeta)\left(\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}v^{\lambda}+e^{\lambda}(\zeta)\partial{}_{\zeta}v^{\lambda}\right),

where we denote

e^{\lambda}(\zeta):=\frac{\partial\Psi^{\lambda}}{\partial\overline{z^{\lambda}}}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta))\left(\frac{\partial\overline{\Psi^{\lambda}}}{\partial\overline{z^{\lambda}}}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta))\right)^{-1},\quad h^{\lambda}(\zeta):=\frac{\partial\overline{\Psi^{\lambda}}}{\partial\overline{z^{\lambda}}}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta)).

Note that since
\Psi^{0}=\Gamma^{0}=id
, we have
e^{0}\equiv 0
and
h^{0}\equiv 1
on
\Omega
. Thus for
|\lambda|
small,
|e^{\lambda}|<1
. Now
v^{\lambda}
is the solution to

(6.86)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}v^{\lambda}+e^{\lambda}(\zeta)\partial{}_{\zeta}v^{\lambda}+A_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)v^{\lambda}(\zeta)+B_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)\overline{v^{\lambda}(\zeta)}=F_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)&\text{in $\Omega$;}\\
Re[\overline{l_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)}v^{\lambda}(\zeta)]=\gamma_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)&\text{on $b\Omega$},\end{cases}

where

A_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)=(h^{\lambda})^{-1}A^{\lambda}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta)),B_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)=(h^{\lambda})^{-1}B^{\lambda}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta)),
and

\displaystyle F_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)=(h^{\lambda})^{-1}F^{\lambda}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta)),\quad l_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)=l^{\lambda}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta)),\quad\gamma_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta)=\gamma^{\lambda}(\Gamma^{\lambda}(\zeta)).

By assumption,
e^{\lambda},A^{\lambda}_{1},B^{\lambda}_{1},F^{\lambda}_{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j}(\Omega^{\lambda})
, and
l_{1}^{\lambda},\gamma_{1}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
.
Subtracting equation (
6.86
) by the same equation with
\lambda
replaced by
\lambda_{0}
and dividing the resulting difference by
\lambda-\lambda_{0}
we get respectively on
\Omega
and
b\Omega

(6.87)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v)+e^{\lambda_{0}}\partial{}_{\zeta}(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v^{\lambda})+A_{1}^{\lambda_{0}}D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v+B_{1}^{\lambda_{0}}\overline{D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v}=F_{2}^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}};\\
Re[\overline{l_{1}^{\lambda_{0}}}D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v]=\gamma_{2}^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}\end{cases}

where we used the notation
D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}f:=(f^{\lambda}-f^{\lambda_{0}})/(\lambda-\lambda_{0})
, and

(6.88)

\displaystyle F_{2}^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}:=D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}F_{1}-(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}e)\partial{}_{\zeta}v^{\lambda}-(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}A_{1})v^{\lambda}-(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}B_{1})v^{\lambda},

(6.89)

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}:=D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}\gamma_{1}-Re[\overline{(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}l_{1})}v^{\lambda}].

In addition,
D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v
satisfies the condition

(6.90)

\begin{gathered}D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v(\zeta_{r})=D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}a_{r}+iD^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}b_{r},\quad r=1,\dots N_{0};\\
D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v(\zeta_{s}^{\prime})=D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}\left\{l(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\cdot)[\gamma(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\cdot)+ic_{s}(\cdot)]\right\},\quad\quad s=1,\dots N_{1}.\end{gathered}

Consider the problem

(6.91)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}u^{\lambda}+e^{\lambda}\partial{}_{\zeta}u^{\lambda}+A_{1}^{\lambda}u^{\lambda}+B_{1}^{\lambda}\overline{u^{\lambda}}=F_{2}^{\lambda}&\text{in $\Omega$; }\\
Re[\overline{l_{1}^{\lambda}}u^{\lambda}]=\gamma_{2}^{\lambda}&\text{on $b\Omega$},\end{cases}

where

(6.92)

\displaystyle F_{2}^{\lambda}:=(\partial{}_{\lambda}F_{1}^{\lambda})-(\partial{}_{\lambda}e^{\lambda})\partial{}_{\zeta}v^{\lambda}-(\partial{}_{\lambda}A_{1}^{\lambda})v^{\lambda}-(\partial{}_{\lambda}B_{1}^{\lambda})v^{\lambda},

(6.93)

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\lambda}:=(\partial{}_{\lambda}\gamma_{1}^{\lambda})-Re[\overline{(\partial{}_{\lambda}l_{1}^{\lambda})}v^{\lambda}].

In addition, we require that
u^{\lambda}
satisfies the condition:

(6.94)

\begin{gathered}u^{\lambda}(\zeta_{r})=a_{r}^{\prime}(\lambda)+i\partial{}b_{r}^{\prime}(\lambda),\quad r=1,\dots N_{0};\\
u^{\lambda}(\zeta_{s}^{\prime})=\partial{}_{\lambda}\left\{l(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)\left[\gamma(\zeta^{\prime}_{s},\lambda)+ic_{s}(\lambda)\right]\right\},\quad s=1,\dots,N_{1}.\end{gathered}

By assumption,
\partial{}_{\lambda}e^{\lambda},\partial{}_{\lambda}A_{1}^{\lambda},\partial{}_{\lambda}B_{1}^{\lambda},\partial{}_{\lambda}F_{1}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k-1+\mu,j-1}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, and
\partial{}_{\lambda}\gamma_{1}^{\lambda},\partial{}_{\lambda}l_{1}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j-1}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
. By the induction hypothesis,
