v^{\lambda}=w^{\lambda}\circ\Gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j-1}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, and there exists some constant
C
independent of
\lambda
such that

(6.95)

\|v^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j-1}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(\|F^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j-1}(\Omega^{\lambda})}+\|\gamma^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j-1}(b\Omega^{\lambda})}\right).

Thus in view of (
6.92
) and (
6.93
) we have
F_{2}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k-1+\mu,j-1}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, and
\gamma_{2}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j-1}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
.
To solve problem (
6.91
) we would like to make a change of coordinates
\psi^{\lambda}:\zeta\mapsto\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}
so that

(6.96)

\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}u^{\lambda}(\zeta)+e^{\lambda}(\zeta)\partial{}_{\zeta}u^{\lambda}(\zeta)=f_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}}}u^{\lambda}_{1}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}),\quad u_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})=u^{\lambda}(\zeta),

for some function
f_{1}^{\lambda}
.
We seek
\psi^{\lambda}
in the form
\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}=\psi^{\lambda}(\zeta)=\zeta+g^{\lambda}(\zeta)
, where
g^{0}\equiv 0
. By (
6.96
) we obtain the following equations for
g^{\lambda}
:

(6.97)

\displaystyle\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}g^{\lambda}(\zeta)+e^{\lambda}(\zeta)\partial{}_{\zeta}g^{\lambda}(\zeta)+e^{\lambda}(\zeta)=0,\quad|e^{\lambda}|_{0}<1,

(6.98)

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})=1+\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}\overline{g^{\lambda}}(\zeta)+e^{\lambda}(\zeta)\partial{}_{\zeta}\overline{g^{\lambda}}(\zeta),\quad\zeta=(\psi^{\lambda})^{-1}(\zeta).

By Theorem 6.4 in
[
2
]
, there exists a solution
g^{\lambda}
to the equation (
6.97
) in the class
\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. Hence
\psi^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. In the following we write
D^{\lambda}=\psi^{\lambda}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
.
From equation (
6.98
) we get
f_{1}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j}(\overline{D^{\lambda}})
. Since
g^{0}\equiv 0
, by (
6.98
) we see that
|f_{1}^{\lambda}|
is nowhere vanishing on
D^{\lambda}
when
\lambda
is small. In view of (
6.96
) and (
6.91
) we then get

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}}}u_{1}^{\lambda}+A_{2}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})u_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}+B_{2}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})\overline{u_{1}^{\lambda}}=F_{3}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})&\text{in $D^{\lambda}$};\\
Re[\overline{l_{2}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})}u_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})]=\gamma_{3}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})&\text{on $bD^{\lambda}$},\end{cases}

where
D^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}
,
A_{2}^{\lambda},B_{2}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j}(\overline{D^{\lambda}})
,
F_{3}^{\lambda}\in C^{k-1+\mu,j-1}(\overline{D^{\lambda}})
,
l_{2}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j}(\overline{D^{\lambda}})
,
\gamma_{3}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j}(\overline{D^{\lambda}})
. The functions are given by

(6.99)

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}),B_{2}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})):=\left[f_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})\right]^{-1}\left(A_{1}^{\lambda}((\psi^{\lambda})^{-1}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})),B_{1}^{\lambda}((\psi^{\lambda})^{-1}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}))\right),

\displaystyle F_{3}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}):=\left[f_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})\right]^{-1}F_{2}^{\lambda}((\psi^{\lambda})^{-1}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})),

(6.100)

\displaystyle(l_{2}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}),\gamma_{3}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})):=\left[f_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})\right]^{-1}\left(l_{1}^{\lambda}((\psi^{\lambda})^{-1}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda})),\gamma_{2}^{\lambda}((\psi^{\lambda})^{-1}(\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}))\right).

By (
6.94
),
u_{1}^{\lambda}
satisfies the estimate

\displaystyle u_{1}^{\lambda}(\psi^{\lambda}(\zeta_{r}))=a_{r}^{\prime}(\lambda)+ib_{r}^{\prime}(\lambda),\quad r=1,\cdots,N_{0};

\displaystyle u_{1}^{\lambda}(\psi^{\lambda}(\zeta_{s}^{\prime}))=\partial{}_{\lambda}\left\{l(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)\left[\gamma(\zeta^{\prime}_{s},\lambda)+ic_{s}(\lambda)\right]\right\},\quad s=1,\cdots,N_{1}.

By the induction hypothesis,
u_{1}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j-1}(\overline{D^{\lambda}})
. Then
u^{\lambda}(\zeta)=u_{1}^{\lambda}\circ\psi^{\lambda}(\zeta)\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j-1}(\overline{\Omega})
, and
u^{\lambda}
is the solution to (
6.91
). Furthermore, in view of (
6.95
) there exists some constant
C
independent of
\lambda
such that

(6.101)

\displaystyle\|u^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j-1}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C\|u_{1}^{\lambda}\|_{C^{k+\mu,j-1}(D^{\lambda})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\left(\|F_{3}^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k-1+\mu,j-1}(\overline{\Omega})}+\|\gamma_{3}^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j-1}(b\Omega)}+\|(a,b,c)\|_{C^{j}}.\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\left(\|F^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})}+\|\gamma^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}(b\Omega^{\lambda})}+\|(a,b,c)\|_{C^{j}}\right).

By the induction hypothesis applied for
j=0
,
v^{\lambda}
satisfies

(6.102)

\|v^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(\|F^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})}+\|\gamma^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})}+\|(a,b,c)\|_{C^{0}}\right).

The induction and thus the theorem will be proved if we show that
u^{\lambda_{0}}
is indeed the
\lambda
derivative of
v^{\lambda}
. Define
G^{\lambda}:=D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v-u^{\lambda_{0}}
. Then in view of (
6.87
) and (
6.91
),
G^{\lambda}
is the solution to the problem

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}G^{\lambda}+e^{\lambda_{0}}\partial{}_{\zeta}G^{\lambda}+A_{1}^{\lambda_{0}}G^{\lambda}+B_{1}^{\lambda_{0}}\overline{G^{\lambda}}=E^{\lambda}&\text{in $\Omega$;}\\
Re[\overline{l_{1}^{\lambda_{0}}}G^{\lambda}]=\phi^{\lambda}&\text{on $b\Omega$},\end{cases}

where

E^{\lambda}(\zeta):=F_{2}^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}(\zeta)-F_{2}^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta),\phi^{\lambda}(\zeta):=\gamma^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}_{2}(\zeta)-\gamma^{\lambda_{0}}_{2}(\zeta).

In addition, by (
6.90
) and (
6.94
),
G^{\lambda}
satisfies the conditions:

(6.103)

\begin{gathered}G^{\lambda}(\zeta_{r})=(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}a_{r}-\partial{}_{\lambda}a_{r})+i(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}b_{r}-\partial{}_{\lambda}b_{r});\\
G^{\lambda}(\zeta_{s}^{\prime})=D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}\left\{l(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\cdot)[\gamma(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\cdot)+ic_{s}(\cdot)]\right\}-\partial{}_{\lambda}|_{\lambda=\lambda_{0}}\left\{l(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)\left[\gamma(\zeta^{\prime}_{s},\lambda)+ic_{s}(\lambda)\right]\right\}.\end{gathered}

Since
e^{\lambda},A_{1}^{\lambda},B_{1}^{\lambda},F_{1}^{\lambda},(f_{1}^{\lambda})^{-1}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\mu,j}(\overline{\Omega})
and
k\geq j\geq 1
, then
\partial{}_{\lambda}e^{\lambda}
,
\partial{}_{\lambda}A_{1}^{\lambda}
,
\partial{}_{\lambda}B_{1}^{\lambda}
,
\partial{}_{\lambda}F_{1}^{\lambda}
are in
C^{\mu,0}(\overline{\Omega})
.
Since
\gamma^{\lambda},l^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\mu,j}(b\Omega)
, then
\partial{}_{\lambda}\gamma^{\lambda}
,
\partial{}_{\lambda}l^{\lambda}\in C^{1+\mu,0}(b\Omega)
. By Proposition
2.3
, we see that
|\partial{}_{\lambda}\gamma^{\lambda}(\lambda_{1})-\partial{}_{\lambda}\gamma^{\lambda}(\lambda_{2})|_{\tau}
converges to
0
as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
, for any
0<\tau<1+\mu
. It follows from (
6.88
), (
6.89
), (
6.92
) and (
6.93
) that

(6.104)

|E^{\lambda}|_{0}\longrightarrow 0,\quad|\phi^{\lambda}|_{\tau}\longrightarrow 0,\quad\text{as $|\lambda-\lambda_{0}|\to 0$.}

As before we can do a change of coordinate
\psi^{\lambda_{0}}:\zeta\mapsto\zeta_{1}
so that in the new coordinates,
G_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1}):=G^{\lambda}((\psi^{\lambda_{0}})^{-1}(\zeta_{1}))
is the solution to the problem

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}G_{1}^{\lambda}+A_{2}^{\lambda_{0}}G_{1}^{\lambda}+B_{2}^{\lambda_{0}}\overline{G_{1}^{\lambda}}=E_{1}^{\lambda}&\text{in $\Omega_{1}$;}\\
Re[\overline{l_{2}^{\lambda_{0}}}G_{1}^{\lambda}]=\phi_{1}^{\lambda}&\text{on $b\Omega_{1}$},\end{cases}

where
A_{2}^{\lambda_{0}},B_{2}^{\lambda_{0}},l_{2}^{\lambda_{0}}
are defined in the same way as expressions (
6.99
) and (
6.100
) with
\lambda
replaced by
\lambda_{0}
, and

\displaystyle E_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1})=\left[f_{1}^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta_{1})\right]^{-1}E^{\lambda}((\psi^{\lambda_{0}})^{-1}(\zeta_{1})),\quad\zeta_{1}\in\Omega_{1};

\displaystyle\phi_{1}^{\lambda}(\zeta_{1})=\left[f_{1}^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta_{1})\right]^{-1}\phi^{\lambda}((\psi^{\lambda_{0}})^{-1}(\zeta_{1})),\quad\zeta_{1}\in\Omega_{1}.

In addition, if we set
\widetilde{\zeta_{r}}=\psi^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta_{r})
and
\widetilde{\zeta_{s}^{\prime}}=\psi^{\lambda_{0}}(\zeta_{s}^{\prime})
, then by (
6.103
)
G_{1}^{\lambda}
satisfies

\displaystyle G_{1}^{\lambda}(\widetilde{\zeta_{r}})=\left\{(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}a_{r}-\partial{}_{\lambda}a_{r})+i(D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}b_{r}-\partial{}_{\lambda}b_{r})\right\}\circ(\psi^{\lambda_{0}})^{-1},

\displaystyle G^{\lambda}(\widetilde{\zeta_{s}^{\prime}})=D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}\left\{l(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\cdot)[\gamma(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\cdot)+ic_{s}(\cdot)]\right\}-\partial{}_{\lambda}|_{\lambda=\lambda_{0}}\left\{l(\zeta_{s}^{\prime},\lambda)\left[\gamma(\zeta^{\prime}_{s},\lambda)+ic_{s}(\lambda)\right]\right\}\circ(\psi^{\lambda_{0}})^{-1}.

In view of estimate (
6.80
), the solution
G_{1}^{\lambda}
satisfies

|G_{1}^{\lambda}|_{0}\leq C\left(\sum_{r=1}^{N}G_{1}^{\lambda}(\widetilde{\zeta_{r}})+\sum_{s=1}^{N^{\prime}}G_{1}^{\lambda}(\widetilde{\zeta}^{\prime}_{s})+|\phi_{1}^{\lambda}|_{\tau}+|E_{1}^{\lambda}|_{0}\right),\quad\tau>0.

As
\lambda\to\lambda_{0}
, the right-hand side converges to
0
.
Hence
|G_{1}^{\lambda}|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega_{1}})}\to 0
, and it follows that
|D^{\lambda,\lambda_{0}}v-u^{\lambda_{0}}|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}=|G^{\lambda}|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}=|G_{1}^{\lambda}|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega_{1}})}\to 0
.
Finally (
6.101
) and (
6.102
) together imply (
6.85
), so the proof is complete.
∎

7.
Appendix

7.1.
Estimates for the Cauchy-Green/
\overline{\partial}
operator

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{C}
. Recall the operator
T_{\Omega}
given by (
2.1
).

Proposition 7.1

(
[
7
, Theorem 1.13]
)
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain. If
f\in L^{1}(\Omega)
, then
T_{\Omega}f
belong to
L^{p}(\widetilde{\Omega})
, for any
1\leq p<2
and for any bounded domain
\widetilde{\Omega}
in
\mathbb{C}
.

Proposition 7.2

(
[
7
, Theorem 1.14]
)
.

If
f\in L^{1}(\Omega)
, then for any
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega)
(i.e. the class of compactly supported smooth functions in
\Omega
), one has the following

\iint_{\Omega}Tf\,\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial\overline{z}}dA(z)=-\iint_{\Omega}f\varphi\,dA(z).

In other words, the equation
\partial{}_{\overline{z}}(Tf)=f
holds in the sense of distribution on
\Omega
.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain. Suppose
f\in L^{p}(\Omega)
, for
2<p<\infty
. Then for
z,z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\left|T_{\Omega}f(z)\right|\leq C(p)\left|f\right|_{L^{p}(\Omega)},\quad z\in\mathbb{C};

\displaystyle\left|T_{\Omega}f(z_{1})-T_{\Omega}f(z_{2})\right|\leq C(p)\left|f\right|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{\alpha_{p}},\quad\alpha_{p}=\frac{p-2}{p}.

Proposition 7.4

(
[
7
, p. 34]
)
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{C}
be a bounded domain. Suppose
\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w\in L^{1}(\Omega)
. Then

w-T_{\Omega}(\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w)=\Phi(z),

where
\Phi
is some holomorphic function in
\Omega
.

Proposition 7.5

(
[
7
, Theorem 1.23]
)
.

Let
2<p<\infty
. Suppose
f
satisfies

M=\int_{\mathbb{D}}\left|f\left(\zeta^{-1}\right)\right|^{p}|\zeta|^{-2p}\,dA(\zeta)<\infty.

Then for
z,z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
, we have

|T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}f(z)|\leq C(p)M
and

|T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}f(z_{1})-T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}f(z_{2})|\leq C(p)M|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{\alpha_{p}},\quad\alpha_{p}=\frac{p-2}{p}.

Combining Proposition
7.4
and Proposition
7.5
one gets

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be the unit disk in the complex plane, and suppose
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{D})
for
2<p<\infty
. Let
P_{n}
be the operator defined by formula (
3.11
):

(7.1)

P_{n}\varphi(z)=T_{\mathbb{D}}\varphi(z)+z^{2n+1}T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}\varphi_{1}(z),

where
\varphi_{1}
is given by the second formula in (
3.11
). Then
P_{n}\varphi\in C^{\alpha_{p}}(\overline{D})
for
\alpha_{p}=\frac{p-2}{p}
, and

|P_{n}\varphi|_{\alpha_{p}}\leq C(p)|f|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{D})}.

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
\Omega\in C^{k+1+\mu}
, where
k
is a non-negative integer, and
0<\mu<1
. Let
P_{n}
be the operator given by formula (
7.1
). Then

|P_{n}\varphi|_{C^{k+1+\mu}(\overline{\mathbb{D}})}\leq C|\varphi|_{C^{k+\mu}(\overline{\mathbb{D}})}.

Proof.

Let
\varphi_{1}(\zeta)=\overline{\varphi(1/\overline{\zeta})}/(\zeta\overline{\zeta^{2}})
and

T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}\varphi_{1}(z)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{D}}\frac{\varphi_{1}(\zeta)}{\zeta-z}\,dA(\zeta).

We have

(7.2)

P_{n}\varphi(z)=T_{\mathbb{D}}\varphi(z)+z^{2n+1}T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}\varphi_{1}(z).

By Theorem 1.32
[
7
, p. 56]
, we have

(7.3)

\displaystyle\partial{}_{\overline{z}}T_{\mathbb{D}}\varphi(z)=\varphi(z),\quad\partial{}_{z}T_{\mathbb{D}}\varphi(z)=\Pi_{\mathbb{D}}\varphi(z),

and
\Pi
is bounded linear operator mapping
C^{k+\mu}(\overline{\mathbb{D}})
to itself. It follows from (
7.3
) that
T_{\mathbb{D}}
is a bounded linear operator mapping
C^{k+\mu}(\overline{\mathbb{D}})
to
C^{k+1+\mu}(\overline{\mathbb{D}})
. To estimate the second operator
T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}\varphi_{1}
. We first extend
\varphi_{1}
to a function in the class
C^{k+\mu}(\mathbb{C})
, by extending
\varphi
to a function in the class
C^{k+\mu}_{0}(\mathbb{C})
.
\varphi_{1}
vanishes in some neighborhood of
0
, and
\varphi_{1}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{C})
.
Write

T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}\varphi_{1}=T_{\mathbb{C}}\varphi_{1}(z)-T_{\mathbb{D}}\varphi_{1}(z).

By
[
7
, p. 63, Thm 1.34]
,
T_{\mathbb{C}}\varphi_{1}\in C^{k+1+\mu}(\mathbb{C})
. Since
T_{\mathbb{D}}\varphi_{1}\in C^{k+1+\mu}(\overline{\mathbb{D}})
, we obtain from the above equation that
T_{\mathbb{D}^{c}}\varphi_{1}\in C^{k+1+\mu}(\overline{\mathbb{D}})
. The proposition follows from (
7.2
).
∎

Proposition 7.8
.

Let
k
be a non-negative integer and
0<\beta<1
. Let
\Omega
be a domain in
\mathbb{C}
with
C^{k+1+\beta}
boundary, and let
\Gamma^{\lambda}:\Omega\to\Omega^{\lambda}
be a
\mathcal{C}^{k+1+\beta,0}
embedding. Let
f^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{k+\beta,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
. Then

\partial{}_{z^{\lambda}}T_{\Omega^{\lambda}}f^{\lambda}
is in
\mathcal{C}^{k+\beta,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, and there exists some constant
C
independent of
\lambda
so that

|\partial{}_{z^{\lambda}}T_{\Omega^{\lambda}}f^{\lambda}|_{k+\beta}\leq C|f^{\lambda}|_{k+\beta}.

The proof follows similarly as in
[
7
, p. 56-61]
.

7.2.
Estimates for Cauchy type operators

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
\Omega^{\lambda}
be a family of bounded domains in
\mathbb{C}
in the class
\mathcal{C}^{1,0}
. That is there is a family of embedding
\Gamma^{\lambda}:\Omega\to\Omega^{\lambda}
such that
\Gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1,0}(\overline{\Omega})
. For
0<\beta<1
, let
f^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta,0}(b\Omega)
, and let
g
be defined by

g(\zeta,\lambda):=P.V.\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f(t,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f(t,\lambda)-f(\zeta,\lambda)}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}+(\pi i)f(\zeta,\lambda).

Then
g\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, and we have

\|g\|_{\beta,0}\leq C_{1,0}\|f\|_{\beta,0}.

Proof.

Let
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\in b\Omega
. Then we have

(7.4)

\displaystyle|g(\zeta_{1},\lambda)-g(\zeta_{2},\lambda)|\leq\int_{|s|<2\delta}\frac{|f(t,\lambda)-f(\zeta_{1},\lambda)|}{|t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}|}+\frac{|f(t,\lambda)-f(\zeta_{2},\lambda)|}{|t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}|}\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\int_{|s|\geq 2\delta}\frac{\overline{f(t,\lambda)}-\overline{f(\zeta_{1},\lambda)}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}}-\frac{\overline{f(t,\lambda)}-\overline{f(\zeta_{2},\lambda)}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}}\,dt^{\lambda}\right|+\pi|f(\zeta_{1},\lambda)-f(\zeta_{2},\lambda)|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{1,0}\|l\|_{\beta,0}\,\delta^{\beta}+\left|\left(\overline{f(\zeta_{2},\lambda)}-\overline{f(\zeta_{1},\lambda)}\right)\int_{|s|\geq 2\delta}\frac{dt^{\lambda}}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{|s|\geq 2\delta}\left(\overline{f(t,\lambda)}-\overline{f(\zeta_{2},\lambda)}\right)\left(\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}}-\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}}\right)\,dt^{\lambda}.

The first term is bounded by
C_{1,0}\|l\|_{\beta,0}\,\delta^{\beta}
.
The second term is bounded by

\displaystyle C_{1,0}\|f\|_{\beta,0}\,\delta^{\beta}\left|log\frac{\Gamma^{\lambda}(2\delta)-\Gamma^{\lambda}(0)}{\Gamma^{\lambda}(-2\delta)-\Gamma^{\lambda}(0)}\right|.

Since
\Gamma\in C^{1,0}(\overline{\Omega^{\lambda}})
, we have for positive constants
c_{1,0}
and
C_{1,0}
,

c_{1,0}=c_{1,0}\frac{2\delta}{2\delta}\leq\left|\frac{\Gamma^{\lambda}(2\delta)-\Gamma^{\lambda}(0)}{\Gamma^{\lambda}(-2\delta)-\Gamma^{\lambda}(0)}\right|\leq C_{1,0}\frac{2\delta}{2\delta}\leq C_{1,0}.

Thus

\left|log\frac{\Gamma^{\lambda}(2\delta)-\Gamma^{\lambda}(0)}{\Gamma^{\lambda}(-2\delta)-\Gamma^{\lambda}(0)}\right|
is bounded uniformly in
\lambda
.
Finally the last term in (
7.4
) is bounded by

C_{1,0}\|f\|_{\beta,0}\,\delta^{\beta}\int_{|s|\geq 2\delta}\frac{|t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}|^{\beta}}{|t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}||t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}|}\,dt^{\lambda}.

Since

|t-\zeta_{2}|\geq|t-\zeta_{1}|-|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|\geq|t-\zeta_{1}|-c_{0}|t-\zeta_{1}|\geq(1-c_{0})|t-\zeta_{1}|
, for some
c_{0}>0
, the above expression is bounded by

\displaystyle C_{1,0}\|f\|_{\beta,0}\,\delta^{\beta}\int_{|s|\geq 2\delta}|t^{\lambda}-\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}|^{\beta-2}\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1,0}^{\prime}\|f\|_{\beta,0}\,\delta^{\beta}\int_{|s|\geq 2\delta}|s|^{\beta-2}\,ds\leq C_{1,0}^{\prime\prime}\|f\|_{\beta,0}\,\delta^{\beta}.

Putting together the results we get

|v(\zeta_{1},\lambda)-v(\zeta_{2},\lambda)|\leq C_{1,0}\|f^{\lambda}\|_{\beta,0}\|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|^{\beta}.\qed

Lemma 7.10
.

Let
\Omega^{\lambda}
be a family of bounded domains in
\mathbb{C}
in the class
\mathcal{C}^{1,0}
. That is there is a bounded domain
\Omega
of
C^{1}
boundary, and a family of embedding
\Gamma^{\lambda}:\Omega\to\Omega^{\lambda}
such that
\Gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1,0}(\overline{\Omega})
. Let
\mathcal{N}
be the operator defined by

\mathcal{N}f(\zeta,\lambda)=\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{\lambda}-\zeta^{\lambda}}\left[v(\zeta,t,\lambda)-v(t,t,\lambda)\right]f(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}.

Suppose
v\in C^{\beta}(\overline{\Omega})
, for
0<\beta<1
, and
|v(\cdot,t,\lambda)|_{\beta}
is bounded by some constant
A_{\beta}
uniformly in
\lambda
and
t\in b\Omega
. Then
\mathcal{N}
maps the space
C^{0,0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
to the space
C^{\beta^{\prime},0}(b\Omega^{\lambda})
, for any
0<\beta^{\prime}<\beta
, and

(7.5)

\|\mathcal{N}f\|_{\beta^{\prime},0}\leq C_{\beta^{\prime}}C_{1,0}\|f\|_{0,0}.

Proof.

First we estimate the sup norm:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{N}f(\zeta,\lambda)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1,0}A_{\beta}|f(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{0}(b\Omega)}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{1}{|t-\zeta|^{1-\beta}}\,dt\leq C_{1,0}A_{\beta}|f(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{0}(b\Omega)},

where the constants are independent of
\lambda
.

Next, we estimate the difference of
\mathcal{N}f
at two points
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\in b\Omega
. Let

n(\zeta,t,\lambda)=v(\zeta,t,\lambda)-v(t,t,\lambda)
. Then
|n(\zeta,t,\lambda)|\leq A_{\beta}|\zeta-t|^{\beta}
.
Writing
\delta=|\zeta-\zeta^{\prime}|
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{N}f(\zeta_{1},\lambda)-\mathcal{N}f(\zeta_{2},\lambda)|=\left|\int_{b\Omega}\left[\frac{n(\zeta_{1},t,\lambda)}{|\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|}-\frac{n(\zeta_{2},t,\lambda)}{|\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|}\right]f(t,\lambda)\,dt^{\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq A_{\beta}|f(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{0}(b\Omega)}\left\{\int_{|\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|<2\delta}\frac{dt^{\lambda}}{|\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|^{1-\beta}}+\int_{|\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|<2\delta}\frac{dt^{\lambda}}{|\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|^{1-\beta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+|f(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{0}(b\Omega)}\int_{|\zeta_{1}-t|\geq 2\delta}\left|\frac{n(\zeta_{1},t,\lambda)}{|\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|}-\frac{n(\zeta_{2},t,\lambda)}{|\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|}\right|\,dt^{\lambda}.

The first two integrals are bounded by
C_{1,0}A_{\beta}^{\prime}|f(\cdot,\lambda)|_{C^{0}(b\Omega)}|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|^{\beta}
. The last integral is bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{|\zeta_{1}-t|>2\delta}\left\{\frac{|n(\zeta_{1},t,\lambda)-n(\zeta_{2},t,\lambda)|}{|\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|}+|n(\zeta_{2},t,\lambda)|\left[\frac{1}{|\zeta_{1}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|}-\frac{1}{|\zeta_{2}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}|}\right]\right\}\,dt^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{1,0}\int_{|\zeta_{1}-t|\geq 2\delta}\frac{|v(\zeta_{1},t,\lambda)-v(\zeta_{2},t,\lambda)|}{|\zeta_{1}-t|}\,dt+C_{1,0}\int_{|\zeta_{1}-t|>2\delta}\frac{|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}||\zeta_{2}-t|^{\beta}}{|\zeta_{1}-t||\zeta_{2}-t|}\,dt

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{1,0}|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|^{\beta}(\log|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|+1)+C_{1,0}|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|\int_{|\zeta_{1}-t|>2\delta}\frac{1}{|\zeta_{1}-t|^{2-\beta}}\,dt

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{1,0}^{\prime}|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|^{\beta^{\prime}}

for any
0<\beta^{\prime}<\beta
. Combining the estimates we get (
7.5
).
∎

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
0<\beta<1
and
k,l\geq 0
be integers. Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{C}
with
b\Omega\in C^{1}
. For
f\in L^{1}(b\Omega)
, define the Cauchy transform
\mathfrak{C}f
on
\mathbb{C}\setminus b\Omega
by

\mathfrak{C}f(z):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f(\zeta)}{\zeta-z}\,d\zeta.

Suppose
f\in C^{\beta}(b\Omega)
. Then
f
extends to a function
\mathfrak{C}^{+}f\in C^{\beta}(\overline{\Omega})
. Moreover on
b\Omega

\mathfrak{C}^{+}f(z)=f(z)+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{f(z)-f(\zeta)}{z-\zeta}\,d\zeta.

(ii) Suppose
b\Omega\in C^{k+1+\beta}
, and
f\in C^{l+\beta}(b\Omega)
with
k+1\geq l
. Then
\mathfrak{C}^{+}f\in C^{l+\beta}(\overline{\Omega})
.

The proof is standard and the reader may refer to
[
1
]
.

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
A,B\in L_{p,2}(\mathbb{C})
. Let
w(z)
be the generalized Cauchy integral with density
\varphi
, defined by

w(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}G_{1}(z,t)\varphi(t)\,dt-G_{2}(z,t)\overline{\varphi(t)}\,\overline{dt},

where
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are the fundamental kernels of the class
\mathcal{L}_{p,2}(A,B,\mathbb{C})
. Suppose
\varphi(t)\in C^{\mu}(b\Omega)
, then
w(z)\in C^{\nu}(\overline{\Omega})
, where
\nu=\text{min}(\alpha_{p},\mu)
,
\alpha_{p}=\frac{p}{p-2}
.

Proof.

We write

G_{1}(z,t)=\frac{\Sigma_{i=1,2}[e^{\omega_{i}(z,t)}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t)}]}{t-z}+\frac{2}{t-z}
and

G_{2}(z,t)=\frac{e^{\omega_{1}(z,t)}-e^{\omega_{2}(z,t)}}{t-z}.
By (
3.24
) and (
3.25
) we have

|e^{\omega_{i}(z,t)}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t)}|\leq C|z-t|^{\alpha_{p}}
for
i=1,2
and
|e^{\omega_{1}(z,t)}-e^{\omega_{2}(z,t)}|\leq C|z-t|^{\alpha_{p}}.

Then

\displaystyle w(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial{}\Omega}\frac{\varphi(t)\,dt}{t-z}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\partial{}\Omega}\frac{\Sigma_{i=1,2}[e^{\omega_{i}(z,t)}-e^{\omega_{i}(t,t)}]}{t-z}\varphi(t)\,dt-\int_{\partial{}\Omega}\frac{e^{\omega_{1}(z,t)}-e^{\omega_{2}(z,t)}}{t-z}\overline{\varphi(t)}\,\overline{dt}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial{}\Omega}\frac{\varphi(t)\,dt}{t-z}+\int_{\partial{}\Omega}\frac{F(z,t)}{|t-z|^{1-\alpha_{p}}}\varphi(t)\,dt+\int_{\partial{}\Omega}\frac{G(z,t)}{|t-z|^{1-\alpha_{p}}}\overline{\varphi(t)}\,\overline{dt},

where
|F(z,t)|\leq C|z-t|
and
|G(z,t)|\leq C|z-t|
, for any
z\in\overline{\Omega}
and
t\in\partial{}\Omega
.
The result then follows from Proposition
7.11
.
∎

Proposition 7.13
.

Let
L
be an arc of the class
C^{1}
and let
0<\beta<1
. Define
w
to be the generalized Cauchy integral with density
\varphi\in C^{\beta}(L)
.

w(z):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{L}G_{1}(z,t)\varphi(t)\,dt-G_{2}(z,t)\overline{\varphi(t)}\,\overline{dt},\quad z\notin L.

Then
w
extends to a continuous function from either side of the arc. Furthermore, let us denote by
w^{+}
and
w^{-}
the limiting values of
w
from the region to the left and right of
L
when going in the positive direction. Then the following formulae hold:

(7.6)

\displaystyle w^{+}(\zeta)=\frac{1}{2}\varphi(\zeta)+w(\zeta),\quad w^{-}(\zeta)=-\frac{1}{2}\varphi(\zeta)+w(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in L,

where

w(\zeta)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\,P.V.\int_{L}G_{1}(\zeta,t)\varphi(t)\,dt-G_{2}(\zeta,t)\overline{\varphi(t)}\,\overline{dt},\quad\quad\zeta\in L.

The first integral on the right-hand side is understood to be the Cauchy principal value, and the second integral converging in the ordinary sense.

Proof.

See formula (14.2)-(14.3) from
[
7
, p. 188]
.
The proof uses Plemelj’s formula and estimate (
3.29
).
∎

Proposition 7.14
.

Let
\Omega
be a domain in
\mathbb{C}
with
C^{1}
and compact boundary. Suppose
A,B\in L^{p}(\Omega)
and
A\equiv B\equiv 0
in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Omega
. Define

w(z):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}G_{1}(z,t,\Omega)\varphi(t)\,dt-G_{2}(z,t,\Omega)\overline{\varphi(t)}\,\overline{dt},

where
\varphi\in C^{\beta}(b\Omega)
for
0<\beta<1
, and
G_{i}
are defined by (
3.26
).
Then
w
satisfies the following integral equation (in fact is the unique solution in
C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})
)

(7.7)

w(z)+T_{\Omega}(Aw+B\overline{w})=\Phi(z),

where

(7.8)

\Phi(z):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\varphi(t)\,dt}{t-z}.

Proof.

We will follow some arguments in
[
7
, p. 183-188]
. By the well-known jump formula for Cauchy integral, we have

\varphi(\zeta)=\Phi^{+}(\zeta)-\Phi^{-}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in b\Omega.

In addition,
\Phi\in C^{\beta}(\overline{\Omega})
.
By (
3.28
),

\partial{}_{\overline{z}}G_{i}(z,\zeta,\Omega)=0

if
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Omega
and
\zeta\in\Omega
.
By (
3.30
),

(7.9)

\partial{}_{\overline{z}}G_{i}(\zeta,z,\Omega)=0,\quad\quad\text{if $z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Omega$, \quad$\zeta\in\Omega$};

w(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}G_{1}(z,t,\Omega)(\Phi^{+}-\Phi^{-})(t)\,dt-G_{2}(z,t,\Omega)\overline{(\Phi^{+}-\Phi^{-})(t)}\,\overline{d{t}}.

By (
7.9
) and Cauchy’s theorem,

\int_{b\Omega}G_{1}(z,t,\Omega)\Phi^{-}(t)\,dt-G_{2}(z,t,\Omega)\overline{\Phi^{-}(t)}\,\overline{dt}=0.

Using this and applying Green’s identity on the domain
\Omega_{\varepsilon}=\{\zeta\in\Omega:|\zeta-z|>\varepsilon\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle w(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}G_{1}(z,t,\Omega)\Phi^{+}(t)\,dt-G_{2}(z,t,\Omega)\overline{\Phi^{+}(t)}\,\overline{dt}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\{\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}\left(\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}G_{1}(z,\zeta,\Omega)\right)\Phi(\zeta)\,dA(\zeta)+\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}\left(G_{2}(z,\zeta,\Omega)\right)\overline{\Phi(\zeta)}\,dA(\zeta)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\{\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|t-z|=\varepsilon}G_{1}(z,t,\Omega)\Phi(t)-\Omega_{2}(z,t,\Omega)\overline{\Phi(t)}\,\overline{dt}\right\},

where we used that
\Phi
is holomorphic.

Taking the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0
, and taking into account estimate (
3.29
), we have

(7.10)

w(z)=\Phi(z)+\iint_{\Omega}R_{1}(z,\zeta,\Omega)\Phi(\zeta)dA(\zeta)+\iint_{\Omega}R_{2}(z,\zeta,\Omega)\overline{\Phi(\zeta)}\,dA(\zeta),

where

R_{1}(z,\zeta,\Omega)=\frac{1}{\pi}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}G_{1}(z,\zeta,\Omega)
and
R_{2}(z,\zeta,\Omega)=\frac{1}{\pi}\partial{}_{\overline{\zeta}}G_{2}(z,\zeta,\Omega).

We now show that expression (
7.10
) represents the unique solution to the integral equation:

(7.11)

w(z)-\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\Omega}\frac{A(\zeta)w(\zeta)+B(\zeta)\overline{w(\zeta)}}{\zeta-z}\,dA(\zeta)=\Phi(z),\quad z\in\Omega.

By Vekua
[
7
, p. 156]
, there exists a unique solution
w_{0}
in the class
L^{q}(\overline{\Omega})
,
q=\frac{p}{p-1}
. Our goal is to show that
w
defined by (
7.10
) is equal to
w_{0}
.

Taking
\partial{}_{\overline{z}}
on both sides we get
\mathcal{L}_{A,B}w=0
in
\Omega
.
Write (
7.11
) as

(7.12)

w_{0}-P_{\Omega}w_{0}=\Phi,\quad\quad P_{\Omega}w_{0}:=-T_{\Omega}(Aw_{0}+B\overline{w_{0}})=T_{\Omega}(\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{0}).

By
[
7
, p. 50]
, there exists an integer
n
so that
P^{n}w_{0}
is in the class
C^{\tau}
for some
\tau>0
. Since
P(\Phi)\in C^{\frac{p}{p-2}}(\overline{\Omega})
(Proposition
7.6
) and

w_{0}=P^{n}w_{0}+P^{n-1}(\Phi)+P^{n-2}(\Phi)+\cdots+\Phi,

we have that
w_{0}\in C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})
. Since
A,B\in L^{p}(\Omega)
, we have
\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{0}\in L^{p}(\Omega)
. Hence by the remark in
[
7
, p.41]
we have

(7.13)

w_{0}-P_{\Omega}w_{0}\equiv w_{0}-T_{\Omega}(\partial{}_{\overline{z}}w_{0})=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{w_{0}(t)\,dt}{t-z}.

Now the left-hand sides of (
7.12
) and (
7.13
) are the same, so the right-hand side are the same as well and we get

\Phi(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{\varphi(t)\,dt}{t-z}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{b\Omega}\frac{w_{0}(t)\,dt}{t-z},\quad\quad z\in\Omega.

It follows then by the jump formula for Cauchy integral that
w_{0}(\zeta)=\Phi^{+}(\zeta)-\Phi^{-}(\zeta)
, for
\zeta\in b\Omega
.
By Proposition
3.2
,
w_{0}
can be represented as

\displaystyle w_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial{}\Omega}G_{1}(z,t,\Omega)w_{0}(t)\,dt-G_{2}(z,t,\Omega)\overline{w_{0}(t)}\,\overline{dt}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial{}\Omega}G_{1}(z,t,\Omega)(\Phi^{+}-\Phi^{-})(t)\,dt-G_{2}(z,t,\Omega)\overline{(\Phi^{+}-\Phi^{-})(t)}\,\overline{d{t}}=w(z).\qed

7.3.
Theory of singular integral operators on curves

In the following we let
L
be a piece-wise, non-intersecting
C^{1}
curve on the plane.

Definition 7.15
.

We call an operator
\mathcal{N}
a
Fredholm
operator if it takes the form

\mathcal{F}\varphi(t_{0})=A(t_{0})\varphi(t_{0})+\mathcal{N}(\varphi)(t_{0}),

where
A
is a function nowhere vanishing on
L
and
\mathcal{N}
is a compact operator on
C^{0}(L)
.

Definition 7.16
.

We call an operator defined by

\mathcal{K}\varphi(t_{0}):=A(t_{0})\varphi(t_{0})+\frac{1}{\pi i}P.V.\int_{L}\frac{K(t_{0},t)\varphi(t)\,dt}{t-t_{0}},\quad t_{0}\in L

a
singular integral operator (with Cauchy type kernel)
.

The functions
A
and
K
are assumed to be Hölder continuous in their respective arguments. We can write
\mathcal{K}\varphi
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\varphi(t_{0})

\displaystyle=A(t_{0})\varphi(t_{0})+\frac{B(t_{0})}{\pi i}\,P.V.\int_{L}\frac{\varphi(t)dt}{t-t_{0}}+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{L}k(t_{0},t)\,dt,

where

B(t_{0})=K(t_{0},t_{0})
and
k(t_{0},t)=\frac{K(t_{0},t)\varphi(t)-K(t_{0},t_{0})\varphi(t_{0})}{t-t_{0}}.

The operator
\mathcal{K}^{0}
defined by

\mathcal{K}^{0}\varphi(t_{0})=A(t_{0})\varphi(t_{0})+\frac{B(t_{0})}{\pi i}\,P.V.\int_{L}\frac{\varphi(t)dt}{t-t_{0}}

will be called the
dominant part
of the operator
\mathcal{K}
.

We define the
adjoint operator
of
\mathcal{K}
by

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}(\psi)(t_{0})=A(t_{0})\varphi(t_{0})-\frac{1}{\pi i}P.V.\int_{L}\frac{K(t,t_{0})\psi(t)\,dt}{t-t_{0}}.

The dominant part of
\mathcal{K^{\prime}}
is

(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})^{0}(\psi)(t_{0})=A(t_{0})\psi(t_{0})-\frac{B(t_{0})}{\pi i}P.V.\int_{L}\frac{\psi(t)}{t-t_{0}}\,dt.

For any two functions
\varphi
and
\psi
which are Hölder continuous on
L
, one has

\int_{L}(\mathcal{K}\varphi)\psi\,dt=\int_{L}\varphi(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\psi)\,dt.

Lemma 7.17

(Composition of singular integral operators)
.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
be the two singular integral operators defined by

(7.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{j}(t_{0})=A_{i}(t_{0})\varphi(t_{0})+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{L}\frac{K_{j}(t_{0},t)\varphi(t)}{t-t_{0}}\,dt,\quad t_{0}\in L,\quad j=1,2.

Then the composition or the product of
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
is

(7.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}(t_{0})

\displaystyle=\left[A_{1}(t_{0})A_{2}(t_{0})+B_{1}(t_{0})B_{2}(t_{0})\right]\varphi(t_{0})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{L}\frac{A_{1}(t_{0})K_{2}(t_{0},t)+K_{1}(t_{0},t)A_{2}(t)}{t-t_{0}}\varphi(t)\,dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(\pi i)^{2}}\int_{L}\left[\int_{L}\frac{K_{1}(t_{0},t_{1})K_{2}(t_{1},t)}{(t_{1}-t_{0})(t-t_{1})}\,dt_{1}\right]\varphi(t)\,dt.

Proof.

See formula (45.9) in
[
5
, p. 119]
.
∎

Denote the double integral in expression (
7.15
) by
\mathcal{F}_{12}(\varphi)
. We claim that
\mathcal{F}_{12}
is a compact integral operator. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle I(t,t_{0})=\int_{L}\frac{K_{1}(t_{0},t_{1})K_{2}(t_{1},t)}{(t_{1}-t_{0})(t-t_{1})}\,dt_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t-t_{0}}\left[v(t_{0},t)-v(t,t)\right]

where we set
F(t_{1},t,t_{0})=K_{1}(t_{0},t_{1})K_{2}(t_{1},t)
, and

\displaystyle v(t_{0},t)=P.V.\int_{L}\frac{F(t_{1},t,t_{0})}{t_{1}-t_{0}}\,dt_{1}=\int_{L}\frac{F(t_{1},t,t_{0})-F(t_{0},t,t_{0})}{t_{1}-t_{0}}\,dt_{1}+(\pi i)F(t_{0},t,t_{0}),

\displaystyle v(t,t)=P.V.\int_{L}\frac{F(t_{1},t,t_{0})}{t_{1}-t}\,dt_{1}=\int_{L}\frac{F(t_{1},t,t_{0})-F(t,t,t_{0})}{t_{1}-t}\,dt_{1}+(\pi i)F(t,t,t_{0}).

Then the double integral becomes

\int_{L}\frac{1}{t-t_{0}}\left[v(t_{0},t)-v(t,t)\right]\varphi(t)\,dt.

By the proof of Lemma
7.9
, we see that
v
is Hölder continuous in the first argument. It follows then by Lemma
7.10
that
\mathcal{F}
is a compact operator.

Denote the single integral in the above product
\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}
by
\mathcal{K}_{12}(\varphi)
. Then
\mathcal{K}_{12}
is a singular integral operator, and we can write it as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{12}\varphi(t_{0})=\frac{A_{1}(t_{0})B_{2}(t_{0})+A_{2}(t_{0})B_{1}(t_{0})}{\pi i}\,P.V.\int_{L}\frac{\varphi(t)}{t-t_{0}}\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{L}\frac{\left[A_{1}(t_{0})K_{2}(t_{0},t)+K_{1}(t_{0},t)A_{2}(t)\right]-\left[A_{1}(t_{0})K_{2}(t_{0},t_{0})+K_{1}(t_{0},t_{0})A_{2}(t_{0})\right]}{t-t_{0}}\varphi(t)\,dt.

The second integral above is clearly a compact integral operator. Then in view of (
7.15
)
\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}
will be a Fredholm operator if one has

(7.16)

0=A_{1}(t_{0})B_{2}(t_{0})+A_{2}(t_{0})B_{1}(t_{0}),\quad t_{0}\in L.

Definition 7.18
.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{2}
be a singular integral operator defined on
C^{\beta}(L)
, for some
0<\beta<1
, and let
\mathcal{K}_{1}
be another singular integral operator such that the composition
\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}
is a Fredholm operator. Then we call
\mathcal{K}_{1}
a
reducing integral operator
of
\mathcal{K}_{2}
.

Given an operator
\mathcal{K}_{2}
with the corresponding
A_{2}
and
B_{2}
, one can choose another operator
\mathcal{K}_{1}
whose corresponding
A_{1}
and
B_{1}
satisfy condition (
7.16
). In particular one can choose
\mathcal{K}_{1}
to be the dominant part of the adjoint of
\mathcal{K}_{2}
, since in this case
A_{1}(t_{0})=A_{2}(t_{0})
and
B_{1}(t_{0})=-B_{2}(t_{0})
.

7.4.
Some other results

The following result is stated and used in Vekua’s proof
[
7
]
and we shall provide the details.

Proposition 7.19
.

Let
w
be a holomorphic function on the unit disk
\mathbb{D}
, continuous up to the boundary circle
S^{1}
, and satisfying the following boundary condition:

Re(z^{-n}w)=\gamma(z),\quad n>0,\quad\text{on $S^{1}$},

where we assume
\gamma\in C^{\beta}(S^{1})
, for
0<\beta<1
. Then

w=z^{n}\mathcal{S}\gamma+\sum_{k=0}^{2n}c_{k}z^{k}
for
z\in\mathbb{D},

where
\mathcal{S}\gamma
is the Schwarz integral

\mathcal{S}\gamma(z):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{S^{1}}\gamma(t)\frac{t+z}{t-z}\frac{dt}{t},\quad z\in\mathbb{D}.

Proof.

Since
w
is holomorphic, one can write
w(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}z^{k}
. Then

z^{-n}w(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}z^{k-n}=\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}z^{j}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}c_{n+j}z^{j}.

Thus on
S^{1}
, one has

\displaystyle Re(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}c_{n+j}z^{j})

\displaystyle=Re(z^{-n}w)-Re(\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}z^{j})=\gamma(z)-Re(\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}z^{j}).

It is easy to see that
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}c_{n+j}z^{j}
is holomorphic in
\mathbb{D}
, and in addition by the assumption the series is continuous up to
S^{1}
. Hence it can be represented as the Schwarz integral of the real part of its boundary value:

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}c_{n+j}z^{j}=\mathcal{S}(\gamma)(z)-\mathcal{S}(Re\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}\zeta^{j})(z),\quad z\in\mathbb{D}.

Multiplying both sides by
z^{n}
we get

(7.17)

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}c_{n+j}z^{n+j}=z^{n}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)(z)-z^{n}\mathcal{S}(Re\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}\zeta^{j})(z),\quad z\in\mathbb{D}.

For
\zeta\in S^{1}
, one has

2Re\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}\zeta^{j}=\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}\zeta^{j}+\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}\overline{c_{n+j}}\zeta^{-j}=\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}\zeta^{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\overline{c_{n-j}}\zeta^{j}.

Let

(7.18)

\varphi(z)=\mathcal{S}(Re\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}\zeta^{j})(z),\quad z\in\mathbb{D},

in other words,
\varphi
is the holomorphic function in
\mathbb{D}
whose boundary value has real part equal to

Re\left(\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}\zeta^{j}\right)
.
Then we claim that
\varphi
takes the form
\Sigma_{k=0}^{n}a_{k}z^{k}
.
Indeed, since
\varphi(z)
is holomorphic in
\mathbb{D}
, it has power series expansion
\varphi=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}a_{k}z^{k}
, with

\displaystyle a_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|t|=1}\frac{\varphi(t)}{t^{k+1}}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|t|=r}\frac{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}t^{j}\,dt+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\overline{c_{n-j}}t^{j}}{t^{k+1}}\,dt,\quad k\geq 0.

For
k>n
, we have
a_{k}=0
, so the claim holds. Substituting (
7.18
) into (
7.17
) we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}c_{n+j}z^{n+j}

\displaystyle=z^{n}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)(z)-z^{n}\mathcal{S}(Re\sum_{j=-n}^{-1}c_{n+j}\zeta^{j})(z),\quad z\in\mathbb{D}

\displaystyle=z^{n}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)(z)-z^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}a_{k}z^{k}=z^{n}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)(z)-\sum_{k=n}^{2n}a_{k}^{\prime}z^{k}.

Finally,
w(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}c_{j}z^{j}+\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}c_{j}z^{j}
implies

w(z)=z^{n}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)(z)+\sum_{n=0}^{2n}c_{k}^{\prime}z^{k},

for some
c_{k}^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition 7.20
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{C}
, and
0<\beta<1
. Suppose
\{f_{n}\}
is a sequence of functions in
C^{\beta}(\overline{\Omega})
satisfying
|f_{n}|_{C^{\beta}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C
and
|f_{n}|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\to 0
. Then
|f_{n}|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}\to 0
.

Proof.

Suppose
|f|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}>\varepsilon_{1}
. Then there exists
x_{0}\in\Omega
such that
f(x_{0})>\varepsilon_{1}
. Since
|f(x)-f(x_{0})|\leq|f|_{\beta}|x-x_{0}|^{\beta}
, we have
|f(x)-f(x_{0})|<\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2}
whenever
|x-x_{0}|<\delta=\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2|f|_{\beta}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}
. Let
B(x_{0},\delta)
be the ball of radius
\delta
centered at
x_{0}
, then for all
x\in B(x_{0},\delta)
,
|f(x)|>\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2}
. Consequently

|f|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\geq\int_{B(x_{0},\delta)}|f(x)|\,dx\geq\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2}\pi\delta^{2}=C_{\beta}|f|_{\beta}^{-\frac{2}{\beta}}\varepsilon_{1}^{\frac{\beta+2}{\beta}},

where
C_{\beta}=\pi 2^{-\frac{\beta+2}{\beta}}
. Note that if
x_{0}
happen to be on the boundary
b\Omega
, then the above estimates hold with an extra constant.

Given
\varepsilon_{0}
, we set
\varepsilon_{1}=C_{\beta}^{\prime}|f|_{\beta}^{\frac{2}{2+\beta}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2+\beta}}
, where
C^{\prime}_{\beta}=C_{\beta}^{-\frac{\beta}{2+\mu}}
. Then by above we see that whenever
|f|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}<\varepsilon_{0}
, we have
|f|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
. By taking
\varepsilon_{0}\to 0
we get the conclusion.
∎

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
\Gamma^{\lambda}:\Omega\to\Omega^{\lambda}
be a
\mathcal{C}^{1+\mu,0}
embedding, where
0<\mu<1
. For
t,t_{0}\in b\Omega^{\lambda}
, let
t^{\lambda}:=\Gamma^{\lambda}(t)
and
t_{0}^{\lambda}:=\Gamma^{\lambda}(t_{0})
. Define

K(t,t_{0},\lambda):=\partial{}_{\tau_{t_{0}^{\lambda}}}\arg(t_{0}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda}).

(i)We have

|K(t,t_{0},\lambda)|\leq C_{1+\mu,0}|t_{0}-t|^{\mu-1}.

(ii) For any
t_{0},t_{1},t_{2}\in b\Omega
with
|t_{1}-t_{0}|\geq 2|t_{1}-t_{2}|
, we have

|K(t_{1},t_{0},\lambda)-K(t_{2},t_{0},\lambda)|\leq C_{1+\mu,0}\,\frac{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}{|t_{0}-t_{1}|^{2-\mu}}.

Proof.

(i) We parametrize
b\Omega
by
\rho(s)
such that
ds
is the arc-length element agreeing with the standard orientation of
b\Omega
. Then
\rho^{\lambda}(s)=\Gamma^{\lambda}\circ\rho
gives a parametrization of
b\Omega^{\lambda}
. Let
t=\rho(s)
and
t_{0}=\rho(s_{0})
. Then
t^{\lambda}=\rho^{\lambda}(s)
and
t_{0}^{\lambda}=\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})
, and we can write
K
as

K(t,t_{0},\lambda)=\partial{}_{\tau_{t_{0}^{\lambda}}}\arg(t_{0}^{\lambda}-t^{\lambda})=\left|\partial{}_{s_{0}}\arg[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s)]\right|.

Denote by
\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})
the outer unit normal vector to
b\Omega^{\lambda}
at
t_{0}^{\lambda}=\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})
. Then we have

(7.19)

\partial{}_{s_{0}}\arg[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s)]=\frac{\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot(\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s))}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s)|^{2}}.

Since

\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot(\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s))=\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot\int_{s}^{s_{0}}(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(r)-(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{0})\,dr

and
\rho^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1+\mu,0}
, the above expression is bounded by
C_{1+\mu,0}|s_{0}-s|^{1+\mu}
. Since
\Gamma^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{1+\mu,0}
, we have
c_{1,0}|s_{0}-s|\leq|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s)|\leq C_{1,0}|s_{0}-s|
. Hence from (
7.19
) we obtain

|K(t,t_{0},\lambda)|\leq C_{1+\mu,0}\frac{|s_{0}-s|^{1+\mu}}{|s_{0}-s|^{2}}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}|s_{0}-s|^{\mu-1}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}^{\prime}|t_{0}-t|^{\mu-1}.

(ii) Using formula (
7.19
) we have

\displaystyle|K(t_{1},t_{0},\lambda)

\displaystyle-K(t_{2},t_{0},\lambda)|=\left|\partial{}_{s_{0}}\arg[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})]-\partial{}_{s_{0}}\arg[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot(\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1}))}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})|^{2}}-\frac{\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot(\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})|^{2}}\right|.

The above expression is bounded by the sum
K_{1}+K_{2}
, where

\displaystyle K_{1}=\frac{1}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})|^{2}}\left|\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})]\right|,

\displaystyle K_{2}=\left|\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})]\right|\left|\frac{1}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})|^{2}}-\frac{1}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})|^{2}}\right|.

We estimate each
K_{i}
. For
K_{1}
we have

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})|^{2}}\left|\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(r)-(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{0})\,dr\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1+\mu,0}\frac{1}{|s_{0}-s_{1}|^{2}}|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{1+\mu}=C_{1+\mu,0}\frac{|s_{1}-s_{2}|}{|s_{0}-s_{1}|^{2-\mu}}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}^{\prime}\frac{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}{|t_{0}-t_{1}|^{2-\mu}},

where we used
|t_{1}-t_{0}|\geq 2|t_{1}-t_{2}|
.
For
K_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle K_{2}=\left|\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})]\right|\frac{\left||\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})|^{2}-|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})|^{2}\right|}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})|^{2}|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})]\right|\frac{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})|\left(|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})|+|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})|\right)}{|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{1})|^{2}|\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})|^{2}},

where the constant
C_{0}
is uniform in
\lambda
. Since

\displaystyle\left|\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot[\rho^{\lambda}(s_{0})-\rho^{\lambda}(s_{2})]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\nu^{\lambda}(s_{0})\cdot\int_{s_{2}}^{s_{0}}(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(r)-(\rho^{\lambda})^{\prime}(s_{0})\,dr\right|\leq C_{1+\mu,0}|s_{0}-s_{2}|^{1+\mu},

we have

K_{2}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}|s_{0}-s_{2}|^{1+\mu}|s_{1}-s_{2}|\left(\frac{1}{|s_{0}-s_{1}|^{2}|s_{0}-s_{2}|}+\frac{1}{|s_{0}-s_{1}||s_{0}-s_{2}|^{2}}\right).
Since
|t_{1}-t_{0}|\geq 2|t_{1}-t_{2}|
, we have
|t_{2}-t_{0}|\geq|t_{1}-t_{0}|-|t_{1}-t_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{2}|t_{1}-t_{0}|
, and

|t_{2}-t_{0}|\leq|t_{1}-t_{0}|+|t_{1}+t_{2}|\leq\frac{3}{2}|t_{1}-t_{0}|
. Then
c_{0}|s_{1}-s_{0}|\leq|s_{2}-s_{0}|\leq C_{0}^{\prime}|s_{1}-s_{0}|
.
Consequently,

K_{2}\leq C_{1+\mu,0}^{\prime}|s_{1}-s_{2}|\frac{|s_{0}-s_{1}|^{1+\mu}}{|s_{0}-s_{1}|^{3}}=C_{1+\mu,0}^{\prime}\frac{|s_{1}-s_{2}|}{|s_{0}-s_{1}|^{2-\mu}}.

Combining the bounds for
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
we get the desired estimate.
∎
